1	# CLAUDE.md - Bot Instructions
2	
3	## Project Structure
4	
5	This project uses **two separate jj-git repos**:
6	
7	1. **App repo** (`/` — project root): Contains the application source code.
8	2. **Bot session repo** (`/.claude/`): Contains Claude Code session data.
9	
10	Both repos are managed with `jj` (Jujutsu), which coexists with git.
11	
12	## Repo Paths (relative from project root)
13	
14	- App repo: `.` (project root)
15	- Bot session repo: `.claude`
16	  (symlink from `~/.claude/projects/<path-to-project-root>/.claude`)
17	
18	## Working Directory
19	
20	Prefer staying in the project root. Use `-R` flags or absolute paths
21	to target other directories rather than `cd`. If `cd` seems necessary,
22	discuss with the user first — losing track of cwd causes subtle
23	command failures downstream.
24	
25	## Committing
26	
27	Use `-R` (`--repository`) at the end to target the correct repo. Use
28	relative paths to reduce noise. Putting `-R` last keeps the verb/action
29	visible at the start of the command.
30	
31	### App repo
32	```
33	jj commit -m \
34	"title" \
35	-m "body
36	
37	ochid: /.claude/<changeID>" \
38	-R .
39	```
40	
41	### Bot session repo
42	```
43	jj commit -m \
44	"title" \
45	-m "body
46	
47	ochid: /<changeID>" \
48	-R .claude
49	```
50	
51	## jj Basics
52	
53	- `jj st -R .` / `jj st -R .claude` — show working copy status
54	- `jj log -R .` / `jj log -R .claude` — show commit log
55	- `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — finalize working copy into a commit
56	- `jj describe -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — set description without committing
57	- `jj git push --bookmark <name> -R <repo>` — push a bookmark (no
58	  `--allow-new` flag; jj pushes new bookmarks without special flags)
59	- In jj, the working copy (@) is always a mutable commit being edited.
60	  `jj commit` finalizes it and creates a new empty working copy on top.
61	- The `.claude` repo always has uncommitted changes during an active
62	  session because session data updates continuously.
63	
64	## Commit Message Style
65	
66	Use [Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/) with
67	a version suffix:
68	
69	```
70	<type>: <short description> (<version>)
71	```
72	
73	- **Title**: target ~50 chars, short summary of *what* changed.
74	  Include the version. Common types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`,
75	  `test`, `docs`, `chore`.
76	- **App-repo body**: short intro paragraph (1–3 sentences), then a
77	  terse bullet list. Each bullet corresponds one-to-one with the
78	  edits structure already documented in `notes/chores-*.md` for
79	  this step — just the file and a one-line gist (e.g.
80	  `README.md: new Overview intro`). Do *not* restate the detail
81	  that lives in chores; the commit body is a scan-able index, not
82	  a duplicate. The chores section is the source of truth.
83	- **Session-repo body**: terse intro + a few session-activity
84	  bullets. Doesn't need to mirror chores since it describes
85	  in-session work, not code changes.
86	- Examples:
87	  - `feat: add fix-ochid subcommand (0.22.0)`
88	  - `fix: fix-ochid prefix bug (0.22.1)`
89	  - `refactor: deduplicate common CLI flags (0.21.1)`
90	
91	## Pre-commit Requirements
92	
93	### User approval
94	
95	Never execute commit, squash, push, or finalize commands without the
96	user's explicit approval. Present changes for review first; only run
97	them after the user confirms. This applies to late changes too —
98	pause for review before squashing into an existing commit.
99	
100	### Review before proposing the commit block
101	
102	After finishing a unit of work, **summarize what changed and stop
103	there**. Do not pre-emptively lay out the Checkpoint-1 commit
104	commands. Wait for the user to signal review is complete before
105	proposing the commit block. Changes during review are the norm,
106	not the exception; proposing commit text too early creates noise
107	and signals that I consider the work done when it usually isn't.
108	
109	This applies per-step in a multi-step flow too — each dev step
110	gets a review pause before its commit block appears.
111	
112	Signals that review is complete include explicit approval ("let's
113	commit", "looks good, commit it") **and any directive to start
114	the next step** ("do dev4", "next", "go dev(N+1)"). In that case
115	the previous step must be committed first — always commit the
116	current step before starting the next; don't ask.
117	
118	### Notes references
119	
120	Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
121	See [Todo format](notes/README.md#todo-format) for details.
122	
123	### Versioning
124	
125	Every change must start with a version bump. See
126	[Versioning during development](notes/README.md#versioning-during-development)
127	for details. Get user approval on single-step vs multi-step before starting.
128	
129	### Chores section headers
130	
131	Chores section headers use trailing version format:
132	
133	```
134	## Description (X.Y.Z)
135	```
136	
137	Example: `## Add `fn claude-symlink` (0.27.0)`
138	
139	### Pre-commit checklist
140	
141	Before proposing a commit, run all of the following and fix any issues:
142	
143	1. `cargo fmt`
144	2. `cargo clippy`
145	3. `cargo test`
146	4. `cargo install --path .` (if applicable)
147	5. Retest after install
148	6. Update `notes/todo.md` — add to `## Done` if completing a task
149	7. Update `notes/chores-*.md` — add a subsection describing the change
150	8. Update `notes/README.md` — if functionality changed (new flags,
151	   new subcommands, changed behavior)
152	
153	## ochid Trailers
154	
155	Every commit body must include an `ochid:` trailer pointing to the
156	counterpart commit in the other repo. The value is a workspace-root-relative
157	path followed by the changeID:
158	
159	- App repo commits point to `.claude`: `ochid: /.claude/<changeID>`
160	- Bot session commits point to app repo: `ochid: /<changeID>`
161	
162	Use `vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L` to get both changeIDs (first line
163	is app repo, second is `.claude`).
164	
165	## Commit-Push-Finalize Flow
166	
167	Two-checkpoint flow with explicit user approval at each stage.
168	
169	**Run this flow after every step** — not only at session end. Single-step
170	and multi-step changes are of equal importance: a single-step change is
171	one flow; a multi-step change is one flow per `-devN` commit plus one
172	for the final release commit. Each step gets its own commits, its own
173	push, and its own finalize — so dev markers land on the remote and in
174	the `.claude` history as they happen rather than being batched until
175	the end.
176	
177	### Checkpoint 1: Commit
178	
179	Prepare both commit commands and **present them for approval**. Use the
180	**same title** for both commits so they're easy to correlate. The body
181	can differ: the app repo body should summarize code changes; the bot
182	session repo body should note what was done in the session.
183	
184	On approval, execute the commits and set bookmarks:
185	
186	```
187	jj commit -m "shared title" -m "app body" -R .
188	jj commit -m "shared title" -m "session body" -R .claude
189	jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .
190	jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .claude
191	```
192	
193	### Checkpoint 2: Push and finalize
194	
195	After commits succeed, **ask the user to approve push and finalize**.
196	On approval, push the app repo and finalize the bot session in a
197	single operation. Say any final words (e.g. "next is ...") **before**
198	executing — nothing should be output after finalize.
199	
200	```
201	jj git push --bookmark <bookmark> -R . && vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash <SOURCE,TARGET> --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
202	```
203	
204	Replace `<bookmark>` with the active bookmark (e.g. `main`,
205	`dev-0.14.0`). Do **not** push `.claude` separately — `finalize`
206	handles that push after squashing trailing writes.
207	
208	### After finalize: stop and wait
209	
210	After `vc-x1 finalize` is launched — **whether mid-session per-step
211	or at session end** — you **MUST NEVER** proceed to a next step,
212	edit files, run tools, or emit any text (prose, recaps,
213	acknowledgements), until the user explicitly directs you to continue.
214	Treat finalize as a hard stop for the whole turn. Any final words
215	(e.g. "next is ...") must be said in the approval prompt *before*
216	executing finalize; the finalize `Bash` call is the last thing in the
217	turn and nothing follows it.
218	
219	This holds even when the next step seems obvious (e.g. "next is
220	dev-N+1" or "now I should bump the version and commit the release").
221	Wait. The user controls cadence — every push+finalize is a checkpoint
222	they may want to inspect, think about, hand off, or take a break at.
223	Auto-proceeding bypasses that checkpoint and produces unwanted
224	writes between finalize and the next explicit instruction.
225	
226	Exceptions to this rule may emerge later but are not authorized
227	at this stage. Until told otherwise, treat as absolute.
228	
229	### Late changes after push
230	
231	If changes are made to the app repo after it has been pushed (e.g.
232	updating CLAUDE.md or memory), the commit is now immutable. Use
233	`--ignore-immutable` to squash the changes in, then re-push:
234	
235	```
236	jj squash --ignore-immutable -R .
237	jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .
238	jj git push --bookmark <bookmark> -R .
239	```
240	
241	### Finalize the .claude repo
242	
243	The **very last action** in a session is to finalize the `.claude` repo.
244	`--squash @,@-` squashes the working copy into the session commit.
245	The delay gives a safety margin against any pending writes. Always use a
246	short relative path for `--repo`.
247	
248	**Nothing should happen after finalize** — no memory writes, no tool
249	calls, no additional output. If any work is done after finalize, run
250	finalize again so the trailing writes are captured.
251	
252	```
253	vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
254	```
255	
256	Do **not** echo or restate the finalize output — the Bash tool
257	already displays it. Any trailing text output creates writes that
258	miss the finalize squash window.
259	
```

92	
93	### User approval
94	
95	Never execute commit, squash, push, or finalize commands without the
96	user's explicit approval. Present changes for review first; only run
97	them after the user confirms. This applies to late changes too —
98	pause for review before squashing into an existing commit.
99	
100	### Review before proposing the commit block
101	
102	After finishing a unit of work, **summarize what changed and stop
103	there**. Do not pre-emptively lay out the Checkpoint-1 commit
104	commands. Wait for the user to signal review is complete before
105	proposing the commit block. Changes during review are the norm,
106	not the exception; proposing commit text too early creates noise
107	and signals that I consider the work done when it usually isn't.
108	
109	This applies per-step in a multi-step flow too — each dev step
110	gets a review pause before its commit block appears.
111	
112	Signals that review is complete include explicit approval ("let's
113	commit", "looks good, commit it") **and any directive to start
114	the next step** ("do dev4", "next", "go dev(N+1)"). In that case
115	the previous step must be committed first — always commit the
116	current step before starting the next; don't ask.
117	
118	### Notes references
119	
120	Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
121	See [Todo format](notes/README.md#todo-format) for details.
122	
123	### Versioning
124	
125	Every change must start with a version bump. See
126	[Versioning during development](notes/README.md#versioning-during-development)
127	for details. Get user approval on single-step vs multi-step before starting.
128	
129	### Chores section headers
130	
131	Chores section headers use trailing version format:
132	
133	```
134	## Description (X.Y.Z)
135	```
136	
137	Example: `## Add `fn claude-symlink` (0.27.0)`
138	
139	### Pre-commit checklist
140	
141	Before proposing a commit, run all of the following and fix any issues:
142	
143	1. `cargo fmt`
144	2. `cargo clippy`
145	3. `cargo test`
146	4. `cargo install --path .` (if applicable)
147	5. Retest after install
148	6. Update `notes/todo.md` — add to `## Done` if completing a task
149	7. Update `notes/chores-*.md` — add a subsection describing the change
150	8. Update `notes/README.md` — if functionality changed (new flags,
151	   new subcommands, changed behavior)
152	
153	## ochid Trailers
154	
155	Every commit body must include an `ochid:` trailer pointing to the
156	counterpart commit in the other repo. The value is a workspace-root-relative
157	path followed by the changeID:
158	
159	- App repo commits point to `.claude`: `ochid: /.claude/<changeID>`
160	- Bot session commits point to app repo: `ochid: /<changeID>`
161	
162	Use `vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L` to get both changeIDs (first line
163	is app repo, second is `.claude`).
164	
165	## Commit-Push-Finalize Flow
166	
167	Two-checkpoint flow with explicit user approval at each stage.
168	
169	**Run this flow after every step** — not only at session end. Single-step
170	and multi-step changes are of equal importance: a single-step change is
171	one flow; a multi-step change is one flow per `-devN` commit plus one
172	for the final release commit. Each step gets its own commits, its own
173	push, and its own finalize — so dev markers land on the remote and in
174	the `.claude` history as they happen rather than being batched until
175	the end.
176	
177	### Checkpoint 1: Commit
178	
179	Prepare both commit commands and **present them for approval**. Use the
180	**same title** for both commits so they're easy to correlate. The body
181	can differ: the app repo body should summarize code changes; the bot
182	session repo body should note what was done in the session.
183	
184	On approval, execute the commits and set bookmarks:
185	
186	```
187	jj commit -m "shared title" -m "app body" -R .
188	jj commit -m "shared title" -m "session body" -R .claude
189	jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .
190	jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .claude
191	```
192	
193	### Checkpoint 2: Push and finalize
194	
195	After commits succeed, **ask the user to approve push and finalize**.
196	On approval, push the app repo and finalize the bot session in a
197	single operation. Say any final words (e.g. "next is ...") **before**
198	executing — nothing should be output after finalize.
199	
200	```
201	jj git push --bookmark <bookmark> -R . && vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash <SOURCE,TARGET> --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
202	```
203	
204	Replace `<bookmark>` with the active bookmark (e.g. `main`,
205	`dev-0.14.0`). Do **not** push `.claude` separately — `finalize`
206	handles that push after squashing trailing writes.
207	
208	### After finalize: stop and wait
209	
210	After `vc-x1 finalize` is launched — **whether mid-session per-step
211	or at session end** — you **MUST NEVER** proceed to a next step,
212	edit files, run tools, or emit any text (prose, recaps,
213	acknowledgements), until the user explicitly directs you to continue.
214	Treat finalize as a hard stop for the whole turn. Any final words
215	(e.g. "next is ...") must be said in the approval prompt *before*
216	executing finalize; the finalize `Bash` call is the last thing in the
217	turn and nothing follows it.
218	
219	This holds even when the next step seems obvious (e.g. "next is
220	dev-N+1" or "now I should bump the version and commit the release").
221	Wait. The user controls cadence — every push+finalize is a checkpoint
222	they may want to inspect, think about, hand off, or take a break at.
223	Auto-proceeding bypasses that checkpoint and produces unwanted
224	writes between finalize and the next explicit instruction.
225	
226	Exceptions to this rule may emerge later but are not authorized
227	at this stage. Until told otherwise, treat as absolute.
228	
229	### Late changes after push
230	
231	If changes are made to the app repo after it has been pushed (e.g.
232	updating CLAUDE.md or memory), the commit is now immutable. Use
233	`--ignore-immutable` to squash the changes in, then re-push:
234	
235	```
236	jj squash --ignore-immutable -R .
237	jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .
238	jj git push --bookmark <bookmark> -R .
239	```
240	
241	### Finalize the .claude repo
242	
243	The **very last action** in a session is to finalize the `.claude` repo.
244	`--squash @,@-` squashes the working copy into the session commit.
245	The delay gives a safety margin against any pending writes. Always use a
246	short relative path for `--repo`.
247	
248	**Nothing should happen after finalize** — no memory writes, no tool
249	calls, no additional output. If any work is done after finalize, run
250	finalize again so the trailing writes are captured.
251	
252	```
253	vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
254	```
255	
256	Do **not** echo or restate the finalize output — the Bash tool
257	already displays it. Any trailing text output creates writes that
258	miss the finalize squash window.
259	
```

> AGENT

Re-reading both CLAUDE.md files thoroughly (they're identical, ~259 lines each), here's what they actually say about memory:

**Direct mentions:** exactly one, in "Finalize the .claude repo" (line 248):
> **Nothing should happen after finalize** — no memory writes, no tool calls, no additional output.

That's a narrow rule about post-finalize, not a general ban.

**Indirect, but decisive:** CLAUDE.md channels all durable knowledge into versioned `notes/` files that are committed to the app repo:
- `notes/todo.md` — in-progress / todo / done
- `notes/chores-*.md` — per-change edit index (commit bullets point here)
- `notes/done.md`, `ideas.md`, `vc-notes.md`, `jj-tips.md`, `README.md`, `bot-convo.txt`

The pre-commit checklist *requires* updating `todo.md` + `chores-*.md` (+ `README.md` for behavior changes) on every step. Commit bodies are explicitly "a scan-able index" pointing at chores. This is the project's long-term memory — versioned, auditable, reviewed per commit.

So memory is empty because:
1. CLAUDE.md never directs writing to the memory system.
2. Everything that *would* be memory (user role, project state, feedback, references, decisions) is already written to `notes/` and the two CLAUDE.md files — and those are authoritative and versioned.
3. Writing to `~/.claude/projects/.../memory/` would duplicate information that already lives in a more reliable place.

The memory system is effectively superseded here by `notes/` + CLAUDE.md.

1	# Ideas
2	
3	Design sketches that aren't yet scheduled work. Each section captures the state
4	of a discussion so it can be picked up later without re-deriving the reasoning.
5	
6	## Tprobe (time probe)
7	
8	### Motivation
9	
10	Rename/refactor of the current `Probe` primitive. Two goals:
11	
12	1. **Production instrumentation, not just benchmarking.** Drop tprobes into
13	   production code to surface bottlenecks and critical paths that weren't
14	   flagged as critical up front.
15	2. **Minimal hot-path overhead.** Record raw TSC, defer math to the report
16	   phase.
17	
18	### Record shape
19	
20	Each tprobe record holds four pieces of information:
21	
22	- `site_id` — identifies the call site (ideally auto-generated at compile time,
23	  see below).
24	- `start_tsc` — TSC reading at scope entry.
25	- `end_tsc` — TSC reading at scope exit.
26	- `userinfo` — opaque application-attached payload. For an actor, the runtime
27	  records one entry per msg-handler invocation and the actor itself fills in
28	  whatever is useful during the report/save phase.
29	
30	Raw TSC + deferred math keeps the hot path to a `rdtscp` pair plus an append.
31	Converting TSC → ns happens once at report time from a calibration reading.
32	
33	### API shape
34	
35	Three access patterns, all built on the same primitive:
36	
37	1. **Auto-scope (preferred where possible).** For constructs with a
38	   well-defined scope — e.g. an actor msg handler — the runtime opens and
39	   closes the tprobe automatically. User code does nothing.
40	2. **Scope guard.** `let _g = tprobe::scope!();` expands to
41	   `Guard(start!())` with `Drop` calling `end`. Correct by construction for
42	   straight-line scopes; the common case when auto-scope doesn't fit.
43	3. **Raw start/end.** `let id: TprobeRecId = tprobe::start!();` … later …
44	   `tprobe::end(id, userinfo)`. Escape hatch for cases the guard lifetime
45	   can't cover: cross-thread close, async, conditional close, **arbitrary
46	   nesting that isn't stack-structured** (e.g. `start1 start2 end1 start3
47	   end3 end2`).
48	
49	start/end are the basic elements; guard and auto-scope are sugar on top.
50	
51	### Id shape — the load-bearing decision
52	
53	`TprobeRecId` is an opaque handle returned by `start()`. It resolves
54	"which scope am I closing?" when multiple scopes are open simultaneously
55	(overlapping, cross-thread, async, non-stack nested). It does **not** by
56	itself enforce "always closed" — a leaked id leaves an open record — which
57	is why the guard is the recommended form when the scope is straight-line.
58	
59	Two candidate implementations:
60	
61	**Option A — slot reservation.** `start()` allocates a buffer slot, writes
62	`start_tsc` + sentinel `end_tsc`, returns `(slot, generation)`. `end(id)`
63	writes `end_tsc` into the slot.
64	
65	- Drain must skip open slots → head-of-line blocking if one scope is slow.
66	- Ring-buffer overwrite of an open slot needs the generation to detect.
67	- Multiple open slots need non-contiguous reservation; without stack
68	  discipline you can't walk a reservation stack.
69	
70	**Option B — id carries start.** `TprobeRecId = (site_id, start_tsc)`, ~16
71	bytes, no slot allocated at start. `end(id, userinfo)` appends one complete
72	record at close time.
73	
74	- Buffer only ever holds complete records — drain is trivial, no
75	  head-of-line blocking, no staleness.
76	- Arbitrary interleaving (non-symmetric nesting) just works: each end is
77	  independent.
78	- Userinfo must be supplied at `end()` time, not incrementally.
79	
80	**Decision: Option B.** Non-symmetric nesting and drain-to-outside both
81	push strongly toward B. A background task forwards the stream out of the
82	process; having it see only complete records makes the forwarding task
83	trivial.
84	
85	### Auto-generated site_id (compile-time, zero user effort)
86	
87	Ideal: the macro captures the call site; the user writes nothing extra.
88	
89	Recipe — const hash of `file!()`/`line!()`:
90	
91	```rust
92	macro_rules! tp_start {
93	    () => { $crate::start(const { $crate::site_hash(file!(), line!()) }) }
94	}
95	```
96	
97	- `site_id` is a `u64` computed at compile time.
98	- No runtime registration, no `OnceLock` branch on the hot path.
99	- For symbolic reports (so output says `foo.rs:123` not `0xabc...`), the
100	  same macro emits `(hash, file, line)` into an `inventory`/`linkme`
101	  distributed slice. Runtime uses the hash; report phase walks the slice to
102	  resolve names. Both are free in the hot path.
103	
104	**Caveat:** `file!()` is stable within a build but may vary between builds
105	(CI vs. local checkout paths). Fine in-process. If cross-build id stability
106	matters (e.g. comparing "site X got slower between v1 and v2"), normalize
107	paths relative to `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR` before hashing.
108	
109	### Stream forwarding
110	
111	A background task drains the tprobe stream and forwards it outside the
112	app. Because Option B guarantees only complete records hit the buffer, the
113	drain is a straight pull-and-forward — no filtering for open slots, no
114	generation checks, no head-of-line concerns.
115	
116	### Open questions
117	
118	- **Buffer policy.** Ring (overwrites oldest — good for "what just
119	  happened?"), bounded + drain (full traces, needs back-pressure story),
120	  or pluggable. Shapes the `record()` failure mode: block, overwrite,
121	  return `Err`?
122	- **userinfo representation.** Generic `<U>` is fast and type-safe but
123	  pins one type per probe. Fixed-size POD slot (`[u8; N]`) keeps records
124	  uniform across actors with different needs, no heap. `Box<dyn Any>` is
125	  dead in the hot path.
126	- **TSC portability.** Invariant TSC is safe on modern x86 if the thread
127	  is pinned (mpsc-2t already does this); unpinned threads see small
128	  backward jumps across cores. Decide whether the primitive abstracts the
129	  time source (x86 `rdtscp`, aarch64 `cntvct_el0`, `Instant` fallback) or
130	  starts x86-only.
131	- **Double-end detection.** Should `end(id)` detect a double-close? Extra
132	  branch in the hot path vs. letting a real bug class survive. Decide now,
133	  not later.
134	- **Leaked ids.** Option B makes leaks cheap (no reserved slot sitting
135	  open) but still loses the record. Worth making `TprobeRecId`
136	  `#[must_use]` and noisily warning in debug builds.
137	
```

1	//! Free-form measurement probe: a named, single-writer histogram
2	//! of hardware tick-counter deltas.
3	//!
4	//! Same shape as [`crate::probe::Probe`], but the caller records
5	//! tick deltas (`ticks::read_ticks() − ticks::read_ticks()`)
6	//! rather than nanoseconds. Skipping the tick→ns conversion at
7	//! record time trims a mul-shift from the hot path; conversion
8	//! to nanoseconds, if desired, is deferred to the report phase
9	//! using [`crate::ticks::ticks_per_ns`].
10	
11	use hdrhistogram::Histogram;
12	
13	use crate::harness::{fmt_commas, fmt_commas_f64};
14	use crate::ticks;
15	
16	const BOUNDARY_PCTS: &[f64] = &[
17	    0.0, 0.01, 0.10, 0.20, 0.30, 0.40, 0.50, 0.60, 0.70, 0.80, 0.90, 0.99, 1.0,
18	];
19	const BOUNDARY_NAMES: &[&str] = &[
20	    "min", "p1", "p10", "p20", "p30", "p40", "p50", "p60", "p70", "p80", "p90", "p99", "max",
21	];
22	
23	/// A named, single-writer histogram of hardware tick-counter
24	/// deltas. Not `Sync`; cross-thread *sharing* is out of scope.
25	/// `Send` so probes can be moved between threads (e.g. returned
26	/// via a `JoinHandle<TProbe>` on shutdown).
27	pub struct TProbe {
28	    name: String,
29	    hist: Histogram<u64>,
30	}
31	
32	impl TProbe {
33	    /// Create an empty probe. Histogram upper bound is 1e12
34	    /// ticks (~250 s at 4 GHz, ~100 s at 10 GHz), 3 significant
35	    /// figures.
36	    ///
37	    /// Exits the process (code 1) if the hardware tick counter
38	    /// isn't usable — see [`crate::ticks::require_ok`].
39	    pub fn new(name: &str) -> Self {
40	        ticks::require_ok();
41	        // Trigger calibration eagerly so the first report() doesn't
42	        // pay for it.
43	        let _ = ticks::ticks_per_ns();
44	        Self {
45	            name: name.to_string(),
46	            hist: Histogram::<u64>::new_with_bounds(1, 1_000_000_000_000, 3).unwrap(),
47	        }
48	    }
49	
50	    /// Record a single sample, in tick-counter deltas. Values
51	    /// of 0 are clamped to 1 since the histogram's lower bound
52	    /// is 1; back-to-back tick reads can produce 0 on fast cores.
53	    pub fn record(&mut self, ticks: u64) {
54	        self.hist.record(ticks.max(1)).unwrap();
55	    }
56	
57	    /// Render a band-table report for this probe. `as_ticks`
58	    /// controls the display unit: `false` converts stored tick
59	    /// deltas to nanoseconds (default for the CLI); `true` shows
60	    /// raw ticks (`-t`/`--ticks`).
61	    pub fn report(&self, as_ticks: bool) {
62	        let sample_count = self.hist.len();
63	        println!(
64	            "  tprobe: {} [count={}]",
65	            self.name,
66	            fmt_commas(sample_count)
67	        );
68	        if sample_count == 0 {
69	            println!();
70	            return;
71	        }
72	
73	        let unit = if as_ticks { "tk" } else { "ns" };
74	        let tpn = ticks::ticks_per_ns();
75	        let conv = |v: u64| -> f64 { if as_ticks { v as f64 } else { v as f64 / tpn } };
76	        let conv_f = |v: f64| -> f64 { if as_ticks { v } else { v / tpn } };
77	
78	        let n_bands = BOUNDARY_PCTS.len() - 1;
79	        let mut band_first = vec![u64::MAX; n_bands];
80	        let mut band_last = vec![0u64; n_bands];
81	        let mut band_count = vec![0u64; n_bands];
82	        let mut band_sum = vec![0u128; n_bands];
83	
84	        let mut cumulative = 0u64;
85	        for iv in self.hist.iter_recorded() {
86	            let value = iv.value_iterated_to();
87	            let count = iv.count_at_value();
88	            let mid_rank = (cumulative as f64 + count as f64 / 2.0) / sample_count as f64;
89	            let idx = BOUNDARY_PCTS[1..]
90	                .iter()
91	                .position(|&b| mid_rank < b)
92	                .unwrap_or(n_bands - 1);
93	            band_first[idx] = band_first[idx].min(value);
94	            band_last[idx] = band_last[idx].max(value);
95	            band_count[idx] += count;
96	            band_sum[idx] += value as u128 * count as u128;
97	            cumulative += count;
98	        }
99	
100	        struct BandRow {
101	            label: String,
102	            first: String,
103	            last: String,
104	            range: String,
105	            count: String,
106	            mean: String,
107	        }
108	
109	        let mut rows: Vec<BandRow> = Vec::new();
110	        for i in 0..n_bands {
111	            if band_count[i] == 0 {
112	                continue;
113	            }
114	            let mean_val = band_sum[i] as f64 / band_count[i] as f64;
115	            let range_raw = band_last[i] - band_first[i] + 1;
116	            rows.push(BandRow {
117	                label: format!("{}-{}", BOUNDARY_NAMES[i], BOUNDARY_NAMES[i + 1]),
118	                first: fmt_commas_f64(conv(band_first[i]), 0),
119	                last: fmt_commas_f64(conv(band_last[i]), 0),
120	                range: fmt_commas_f64(conv(range_raw), 0),
121	                count: fmt_commas(band_count[i]),
122	                mean: fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(mean_val), 0),
123	            });
124	        }
125	
126	        let label_w = rows
127	            .iter()
128	            .map(|r| r.label.len())
129	            .max()
130	            .unwrap_or(0)
131	            .max("stdev min-p99".len());
132	        let first_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.first.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
133	        let last_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.last.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
134	        let range_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.range.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
135	        let count_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.count.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
136	        let mean_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.mean.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
137	
138	        const INDENT: &str = "    ";
139	        const GAP: &str = "    ";
140	
141	        let first_col = INDENT.len() + label_w + 1 + first_w;
142	        let unit_len = 1 + unit.len(); // " ns" or " tk"
143	        let last_gap = unit_len + GAP.len() + last_w;
144	        let range_gap = unit_len + GAP.len() + range_w;
145	        let count_gap = unit_len + GAP.len() + count_w;
146	        let mean_gap = GAP.len() + mean_w;
147	        println!(
148	            "{:>first_col$}{:>last_gap$}{:>range_gap$}{:>count_gap$}{:>mean_gap$}",
149	            "first", "last", "range", "count", "mean",
150	        );
151	
152	        for r in &rows {
153	            println!(
154	                "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>first_w$} {unit}{GAP}{:>last_w$} {unit}{GAP}{:>range_w$} {unit}{GAP}{:>count_w$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}",
155	                r.label, r.first, r.last, r.range, r.count, r.mean,
156	            );
157	        }
158	
159	        let hist_mean = self.hist.mean();
160	        let skip = first_w
161	            + unit_len
162	            + GAP.len()
163	            + last_w
164	            + unit_len
165	            + GAP.len()
166	            + range_w
167	            + unit_len
168	            + GAP.len()
169	            + count_w;
170	        println!(
171	            "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}",
172	            "mean",
173	            "",
174	            fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(hist_mean), 0),
175	        );
176	        println!(
177	            "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}",
178	            "stdev",
179	            "",
180	            fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(self.hist.stdev()), 0),
181	        );
182	
183	        let trim_count: u64 = band_count[..n_bands - 1].iter().sum();
184	        if trim_count > 0 {
185	            let trim_sum: u128 = band_sum[..n_bands - 1].iter().sum();
186	            let trim_mean = trim_sum as f64 / trim_count as f64;
187	
188	            let mut trim_var_sum = 0.0f64;
189	            let mut trim_var_count = 0u64;
190	            let mut cum = 0u64;
191	            for iv in self.hist.iter_recorded() {
192	                let value = iv.value_iterated_to();
193	                let count = iv.count_at_value();
194	                let mid_rank = (cum as f64 + count as f64 / 2.0) / sample_count as f64;
195	                let idx = BOUNDARY_PCTS[1..]
196	                    .iter()
197	                    .position(|&b| mid_rank < b)
198	                    .unwrap_or(n_bands - 1);
199	                if idx < n_bands - 1 {
200	                    let diff = value as f64 - trim_mean;
201	                    trim_var_sum += diff * diff * count as f64;
202	                    trim_var_count += count;
203	                }
204	                cum += count;
205	            }
206	            let trim_stdev = if trim_var_count > 1 {
207	                (trim_var_sum / trim_var_count as f64).sqrt()
208	            } else {
209	                0.0
210	            };
211	
212	            println!(
213	                "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}",
214	                "mean min-p99",
215	                "",
216	                fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(trim_mean), 0),
217	            );
218	            println!(
219	                "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}",
220	                "stdev min-p99",
221	                "",
222	                fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(trim_stdev), 0),
223	            );
224	        }
225	        println!();
226	    }
227	}
228	
```

1	# Notes
2	
3	This directory contains various notes and documentation related to the project.
4	Each file is organized by topic for easy reference.
5	
6	By default there are chores-*.md and todo.md. Chores are general notes
7	about tasks and todo.md contains short term tasks and their status.
8	
9	In the future we I expect we may want to create a "notes"
10	database to better manage the information, TBD.
11	
12	Examples chore file:
13	```
14	# Chores-01.md
15	
16	General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
17	more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
18	how and why of a particular chore. The section header is short and sweet
19	and the title is appended with the version number of the app when the chore
20	is completed.
21	
22	## Create an app that does something interesting (0.1.0)
23	
24	The app counts from 1 to 100, not to interesting.
25	```
26	
27	## Commit Workflow
28	
29	This project uses a per-step commit / push / finalize flow against
30	two paired repos (app + bot session). Full rules live in
31	[CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md#commit-push-finalize-flow) — that file is
32	the source of truth (the bot loads it automatically; humans can
33	read it from there too).
34	
35	## jj tips
36	
37	For users new to jj see [jj-tips.md](jj-tips.md).
38	
39	```
40	## Chores format
41	
42	Filename: "chores-XX.md"
43	example: chores-01.md
44	
45	Format of section labels: "## <short description> (X.Y.Z)"
46	example: "## Topic format description (0.1.0)"
47	
48	Example chore file:
49	```
50	# Chores-01.md
51	 
52	General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
53	more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
54	how and why of a particular chore.
55	
56	## Do something (1.3.1)
57	
58	Describe something
59	```
60	
61	## Versioning during development
62	
63	This is using jujustiu, jj + git and we'll see how it goes. Below is my
64	git workflow, jj will be different but we'll have to discover that as
65	we go.
66	
67	Every plan must start with a version bump. Choose the approach based on scope:
68	
69	- **Single-step** (recommended for mechanical/focused changes): bump directly to
70	  `X.Y.Z`, implement in one commit. Simpler history.
71	- **Multi-step** (for exploratory/large changes): bump to `X.Y.Z-devN`, implement
72	  across multiple commits, final commit removes `-devN`.
73	
74	The plan should recommend one approach and get user approval before starting.
75	
76	For multi-step:
77	1. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-devN` with a plan and commit as a chore marker
78	2. Implement in one or more `-devN` commits (bump N as needed)
79	3. Final commit removes `-devN`, updates todo/chores — this is the "done" marker
80	
81	The final release commit (without `-devN`) signals completion rather than amending
82	prior commits. This keeps the git history readable and makes it easy to see which
83	commits were exploratory vs final.
84	
85	**Flesh out chores-*.md incrementally per `-devN`.** When a
86	multi-step plan starts, write the full chores section only for
87	the current `-devN`; list the remaining steps as a one-line
88	preview paragraph at the end. As each subsequent step starts,
89	fill in its own detailed section (with its edits, findings, and
90	checkmarks). The plan evolves as work happens, and speculative
91	detail for later steps usually needs rewriting by the time we
92	get there. See the `0.6.0` calibration block in
93	`chores-02.md` — dev1..dev6 were filled in progressively, not
94	planned in full at dev1.
95	
96	## Todo format
97	
98	Todo.md contains two main sections "Todo" and "Done" each item is a
99	short explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more
100	references.
101	
102	Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
103	In markdown, `[2,3]` is a single ref key (won't resolve) and `[2][3]`
104	is parsed as display text `2` with ref key `3` (so `[2]` won't resolve).
105	
106	**Incremental `-devN` updates.** In a multi-step `-devN` flow,
107	each devN commit must also update this file: strike the entry
108	from `## In Progress`, add a bullet under `## Done` with a
109	`[N]`-style link pointing to the devN section in chores. Don't
110	batch the moves into the final release commit — the todo file
111	should reflect what's actually done as each step lands.
112	
113	**Reference-list ordering.** Keep the `[N]`-style references at
114	the bottom of `todo.md` / `done.md` in ascending numeric order —
115	new entries are appended. This preserves chronological context
116	and avoids churn in the diff when entries are added.
117	
118	Examples:
119	
120	# Todo
121	
122	- Add new feature X [details](chores-01.md#feature-x)
123	- Fix bug Y [1]
124	
125	# Done
126	
127	- Fixed issue Z [2],[3]
128	
129	[1]: chores-01bugs.md#bug-y
130	
```

1	# Chores-02
2	
3	Continuation of `chores-01.md`, which crossed 500 lines. Same format;
4	see [Chores format](README.md#chores-format).
5	
6	## Calibration robustness (0.6.0)
7	
8	Calibration in `overhead.rs` occasionally produces a framing value
9	exactly 2× the typical — e.g. 11.11 ns one run, 22.22 ns the next,
10	on `iiac-perf mpsc-2t --pin 5,10 -D 1` (3900X). Not a physical 2×
11	effect — it's the arithmetic of the two-point fit amplifying ~10 ns
12	of endpoint noise.
13	
14	### Analysis
15	
16	Two-point fit:
17	
18	```
19	framing  = min_low − N_LOW · (min_high − min_low) / (N_HIGH − N_LOW)
20	d(framing)/d(min_low)  = N_HIGH / (N_HIGH − N_LOW) = 1000 / 900 ≈ 1.11
21	d(framing)/d(min_high) = −N_LOW / (N_HIGH − N_LOW) = −100 / 900 ≈ −0.11
22	```
23	
24	Reverse-engineering two observed runs (3900X, `--pin 5,10`, `-D 1`):
25	
26	- Run A: `min_low = 60 ns, min_high = 500 ns` → framing 11.11 ns,
27	  loop/iter 0.4889 ns.
28	- Run B: `min_low = 70 ns, min_high = 500 ns` → framing 22.22 ns,
29	  loop/iter 0.4778 ns.
30	
31	Only `min_low` moved (+10 ns). The fit amplified that by 10/9 = 1.11
32	→ +11.11 ns on framing. 11.11 + 11.11 = 22.22. The "exact 2×" is
33	numerology; the real defect is ~10 ns of slop on `min_low`.
34	
35	Why `min_low` floats but `min_high` is stable: `measure(N_LOW=100)`
36	runs first, right after a mere 1,000-iter warmup (~500 µs of work).
37	That's too short to guarantee the CPU has reached peak boost. By the
38	time `measure(N_HIGH=1000)` runs, boost is fully ramped, so
39	`min_high` reliably hits its ~500 ns floor.
40	
41	### --pin vs unpinned main thread
42	
43	`main.rs` already pins the main thread to `pin_cores.first()` before
44	calibration. Measured on 3900X, `-d 3`:
45	
46	| configuration             | framing    | stdev      | p99.99 tail |
47	|---------------------------|-----------:|-----------:|------------:|
48	| `--pin 5,10`              |  11.11 ns  |    787 ns  |    272 µs   |
49	| no `--pin` (main unpinned)|  27.78 ns  | 12,967 ns  |    5.5 ms   |
50	
51	So when `--pin` is omitted, calibration itself inherits the unpinned
52	jitter (migrations, frequency wobble, CCX changes), and the framing
53	number is clearly elevated beyond the true floor. The adjustment we
54	subtract is then derived from a different measurement regime than the
55	one we want.
56	
57	### Should main be unpinned after calibration?
58	
59	Functionally irrelevant. When bench threads run, the main thread
60	blocks in `pthread_join` — it's parked, not runnable, so its affinity
61	doesn't compete with anything. Keeping or dropping the pin changes
62	nothing observable.
63	
64	The cleaner contract is:
65	
66	- Calibration always runs pinned (stable environment, reliable
67	  framing/loop numbers).
68	- Bench runs per `--pin` (user's requested environment).
69	- Main's pinning during the bench phase is a don't-care.
70	
71	So: leave main's pin state alone after calibration; don't add a
72	ceremonial unpin step unless a concrete reason emerges later.
73	
74	### Recommended changes
75	
76	1. **Pin main for calibration regardless of `--pin`.** Even when the
77	   user didn't specify `--pin`, briefly pin main (to `pin_cores[0]`
78	   if set, otherwise a fixed default such as core 0, or a new
79	   `--cal-pin` flag) so calibration always runs in a stable
80	   environment. Biggest coherence win — calibration stops depending
81	   on how the bench is configured.
82	
83	   Add a `--no-pin-cal` flag that reverts to the pre-0.6.0 behavior
84	   (skip the calibration-time pin, so main stays unpinned when
85	   `--pin` is absent). Makes A/B comparison easy — run the same
86	   bench with and without `--no-pin-cal` to see the calibration
87	   difference directly, and gives a quick escape hatch if the new
88	   default ever misbehaves on someone else's box.
89	
90	   **Resolved in dev6** — semantics of `--pin` + `--no-pin-cal`:
91	   `--no-pin-cal` now always wins, regardless of `--pin`. With
92	   `--pin 5,10 --no-pin-cal`, bench threads still pin to 5,10 but
93	   calibration runs with main unpinned (affinity = full startup
94	   mask). Lets the user decouple the calibration environment from
95	   the bench pinning pool when they want the bench pool fixed but
96	   the cal free-running.
97	2. **Longer warmup.** Bump `CAL_WARMUP` from 1,000 to ~100,000
98	   iterations so boost ramp completes before `measure(N_LOW)` runs.
99	3. **More samples.** Bump `CAL_SAMPLES` from 10,000 to ~100,000 so
100	   the min-of-samples estimator reliably hits the theoretical floor.
101	4. **Wider N spread.** Raise `N_HIGH` to ~10,000 (or drop `N_LOW` to
102	   ~10). This drops the noise-amplification coefficient on `min_low`
103	   from 1.11× to ~1.01×, so any remaining endpoint noise barely
104	   perturbs framing.
105	5. *(Optional)* **Sanity-check retry.** Run calibration 3–5 times;
106	   require framing/loop to agree within a tolerance. Retry or warn
107	   otherwise. Belt-and-suspenders for the rare bad run.
108	
109	### Interpretation note: the low min-p1 / p1-p10 bands are real
110	
111	Unpinned `mpsc-2t` runs show a very low `min-p1` band — e.g. `first
112	= 240 ns, last = 6,003 ns` in the 3s unpinned run above, while the
113	pinned `--pin 5,10` run's `min-p1` sits at `7,935…8,175 ns`. That
114	gap isn't calibration noise or a measurement artifact.
115	
116	When both round-trip endpoints stay hot (scheduler co-locates them
117	on the same CCX, neither parks in a futex, both spin on the
118	channel), `std::sync::mpsc` — `crossbeam-channel` under the hood on
119	modern Rust — can round-trip in well under a microsecond. Pinning
120	to `5,10` on a 3900X likely forces cross-CCX placement (cores 5 and
```

373	## CLAUDE.md governance model (0.7.1)
374	
375	Design discussion captured for later cogitation; not implemented.
376	Much of this is bot-workflow infrastructure and may live outside
377	this crate's repo entirely when implemented.
378	
379	### Origin
380	
381	During the 0.7.0 wrap-up, noticed that when corrected on commit
382	style the bot's reflex had been to save a feedback memory rather
383	than re-consult CLAUDE.md — even though the guidance was already
384	there. Having text in the system prompt ≠ reliably retrieving
385	the right section when it applies. The discussion evolved from
386	"why did the bot ignore CLAUDE.md?" into "how should CLAUDE.md
387	itself be organized across user/global + project scopes?"
388	
389	### Observations
390	
391	- Both `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` (global) and `<repo>/CLAUDE.md`
392	  (project) are auto-loaded into the bot's system prompt at
393	  session start; they arrive together, not sequentially.
394	- The current project CLAUDE.md is mostly universal conventions
395	  (dual-repo, ochid trailers, Conventional Commits + version
396	  suffix, pre-commit checklist, commit-push-finalize flow) and
397	  very little project-specific content.
398	- `notes/` references in current CLAUDE.md describe *how to use*
399	  notes, not pointers at specific project files — so the file as
400	  written is almost entirely general.
401	- `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-path>` is a symlink to
402	  `<repo>/.claude`, so `memory/` is versioned inside the bot
403	  session repo (jj-managed) — not a truly private agent

1136	## Arch-neutral ticks module + CPUID invariant TSC (0.8.0-dev5)
1137	
1138	Single-step refactor. Prepares the probe layer for ARM and
1139	RISC-V by moving the hardware-counter plumbing behind a small
1140	arch-neutral API, and replaces the Linux-specific
1141	`/proc/cpuinfo` invariant-TSC check with a direct CPUID read.
1142	
1143	### Motivation
1144	
1145	Dev4 implemented `_rdtsc()` + a `/proc/cpuinfo` `constant_tsc`
1146	check inline: the TSC specificity leaked into `src/tprobe.rs`,
1147	`src/benches/tp_pc.rs`, and `src/tsc.rs`. The user wants to
1148	eventually run this on ARM and RISC-V, both of which have
1149	architecturally invariant counters (`CNTVCT_EL0` and the
1150	`time` CSR) but neither of which has a `rdtsc` instruction or
1151	a `/proc/cpuinfo constant_tsc` flag.
1152	
1153	Dev5 establishes the abstraction and cleans up the x86 impl,
1154	without yet implementing the non-x86 paths.
1155	
1156	### Shape
1157	
1158	- **`src/ticks.rs`** — public API:
1159	  - `read_ticks() -> u64`
1160	  - `ticks_per_ns() -> f64`
1161	  - `require_ok()`
1162	
1163	  Dispatches to a private `mod x86_64;` behind
1164	  `#[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]`. On any other `target_arch`,
1165	  `compile_error!` surfaces a clear message directing the reader
1166	  to add a per-arch impl (AArch64 → `CNTVCT_EL0`; RISC-V →
1167	  `time` CSR).
1168	
1169	- **`src/ticks/x86_64.rs`** — x86_64 impl:
1170	  - `read_ticks()` → `core::arch::x86_64::_rdtsc()`.
1171	  - `ticks_per_ns()` → 10 ms spin-loop calibration (unchanged
1172	    from dev4; lives here now instead of in `tprobe.rs`).
1173	  - `require_ok()` → CPUID `0x80000007:EDX[bit 8]`
1174	    (invariant-TSC bit; both Intel and AMD) **and**
1175	    `minstant::is_tsc_available()` (kernel agrees). Direct,
1176	    OS-agnostic replacement for the dev4 `/proc/cpuinfo`
1177	    string match.
1178	
1179	### CPUID details
1180	
1181	- Leaf `0x80000000` → `EAX` = max supported extended leaf.
1182	  Must be ≥ `0x80000007` or invariant-TSC can't be reported.
1183	- Leaf `0x80000007` → `EDX[bit 8]` = invariant-TSC bit.
1184	- `core::arch::x86_64::__cpuid` is safe on x86_64 in current
1185	  Rust (target architecture unconditionally supports CPUID);
1186	  no `unsafe` block needed.
1187	
1188	### Edits
1189	
1190	- `Cargo.toml` — version bump to `0.8.0-dev5`.
1191	- `src/tsc.rs` — removed.
1192	- `src/ticks.rs` — new dispatch module.
1193	- `src/ticks/x86_64.rs` — new x86_64 impl (rdtsc, calibration,
1194	  CPUID invariant-TSC check).
1195	- `src/probe.rs` — `Probe::new` calls
1196	  `crate::ticks::require_ok()`.
1197	- `src/tprobe.rs` — drops local calibration + direct
1198	  `_rdtsc()`; uses `ticks::require_ok`, `ticks::ticks_per_ns`.
1199	- `src/benches/tp_pc.rs` — drops local `rdtsc()` helper; uses
1200	  `ticks::read_ticks()`.
1201	- `src/main.rs` — `mod tsc` → `mod ticks`.
1202	- `notes/todo.md` — dev5 Done entry.
1203	- `notes/chores-02.md` — this section.
1204	
1205	### Observations
1206	
1207	- **Smoke test matches dev4.** `tp-pc -d 0.3` (3900X, idle):
1208	  `mean min-p99 ≈ 7,290 ns`, `-t` gives `~29,967 tk`, ratio
1209	  ~4.11 tk/ns — consistent with a boosted 3900X and within
1210	  the run-to-run variance of dev4's 7,370 ns / 3.76 tk/ns
1211	  reading. No behavioral regression.
1212	- **CPUID check is stricter than `/proc/cpuinfo`.** The file
1213	  lookup silently succeeded if the `constant_tsc` token was
1214	  present in the flags line regardless of whether the kernel
1215	  trusted it. The CPUID check asks the hardware directly, and
1216	  we keep the `minstant::is_tsc_available()` check alongside
1217	  so kernel rejection still bails. Both must pass.
1218	- **Non-x86 builds fail at compile time, not runtime.** The
1219	  `compile_error!` is deliberate — silently succeeding on an
1220	  arch with no impl would either panic at runtime on a
1221	  missing intrinsic or produce meaningless measurements.
1222	
1223	### Intentionally out of scope for dev5
1224	
1225	- **`CPUID.15h` frequency readout** to skip the 10 ms
1226	  calibration on Intel. Requires a separate fallback when
1227	  `EBX == 0` (denominator not reported) and still needs
1228	  calibration on AMD, so a second follow-up.
1229	- **AArch64 / RISC-V impls.** Structure is ready; filling
1230	  them in wants actual hardware for validation.
1231	- **Framing-adjustment column on `TProbe::report`** —
1232	  outstanding since dev4.
1233	
```

```
375	Design discussion captured for later cogitation; not implemented.
376	Much of this is bot-workflow infrastructure and may live outside
377	this crate's repo entirely when implemented.
378	
379	### Origin
380	
381	During the 0.7.0 wrap-up, noticed that when corrected on commit
382	style the bot's reflex had been to save a feedback memory rather
383	than re-consult CLAUDE.md — even though the guidance was already
384	there. Having text in the system prompt ≠ reliably retrieving
385	the right section when it applies. The discussion evolved from
386	"why did the bot ignore CLAUDE.md?" into "how should CLAUDE.md
387	itself be organized across user/global + project scopes?"
388	
389	### Observations
390	
391	- Both `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` (global) and `<repo>/CLAUDE.md`
392	  (project) are auto-loaded into the bot's system prompt at
393	  session start; they arrive together, not sequentially.
394	- The current project CLAUDE.md is mostly universal conventions
395	  (dual-repo, ochid trailers, Conventional Commits + version
396	  suffix, pre-commit checklist, commit-push-finalize flow) and
397	  very little project-specific content.
398	- `notes/` references in current CLAUDE.md describe *how to use*
399	  notes, not pointers at specific project files — so the file as
400	  written is almost entirely general.
401	- `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-path>` is a symlink to
402	  `<repo>/.claude`, so `memory/` is versioned inside the bot
403	  session repo (jj-managed) — not a truly private agent
404	  scratchpad. This doesn't change the "prefer CLAUDE.md over
405	  memory/" guidance, since CLAUDE.md sits in the app repo and
406	  is visible to all collaborators.
407	
408	### Proposed design
409	
410	Three synchronized masters of a general CLAUDE.md, plus one
411	project-specific extension:
412	
413	- `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` — general master on the local machine
414	- `git@github.com:winksaville/<repo>/CLAUDE.md` — versioned
415	  master (the source of truth)
416	- `<repo>/CLAUDE.md` — byte-identical copy of the master,
417	  dropped in by `vc-x1 init` at project creation and refreshed
418	  via `vc-x1 sync-claude-md`
419	- `<repo>/notes/BOT-PROJECT.md` — project-specific bot
420	  instructions, imported into the bot's context by putting
421	  `@notes/BOT-PROJECT.md` at the bottom of `<repo>/CLAUDE.md`
422	
423	Drift policy:
424	
425	- Warning-only, never auto-update. Auto-update could clobber
426	  intentional local overrides.
427	- Byte-for-byte compare of the three CLAUDE.md files (trivially
428	  simple — no marker parsing, no partial-match logic).
429	- `BOT-PROJECT.md` is project-local and free to vary.
430	- Trigger the check from a `SessionStart` hook running a
431	  `vc-x1 check-claude-md` subcommand — deterministic, doesn't
432	  rely on bot discipline.
433	
434	### Why not sync markers in a single file
435	
436	An earlier iteration considered wrapping the "general" block
437	with `<!-- sync:begin -->` / `<!-- sync:end -->` markers inside
438	one CLAUDE.md per repo, letting project-specific content append
439	below. Rejected because:
440	
441	- Exact-match on the whole file is simpler to reason about and
442	  to implement in tooling.
443	- Easy to accidentally edit inside the marker and create
444	  spurious drift warnings for legitimate reasons.
445	- Overloads CLAUDE.md with two concerns (general + project).
446	
447	The strict-separation model keeps CLAUDE.md meaning exactly
448	one thing and pushes project variance into a clearly-named
449	neighbor file.
450	
451	### Naming
452	
453	`notes/BOT-PROJECT.md` — reads as "bot-facing, project-specific."
454	Alternatives considered: `BOT.md` (less explicit about project
455	scope), `PROJECT.md` (loses the bot-facing signal),
456	`notes/CLAUDE.md` (overloads the name CLAUDE.md which is doing
457	specific work in the three-file sync story).
458	
459	### Open questions
460	
461	- Is `notes/BOT-PROJECT.md` the right location, or should it
462	  live at `.claude/BOT-PROJECT.md` (bot repo) instead?
463	- Does the GitHub master live in a dedicated repo, and does it
464	  carry other per-user dotfiles too, or only CLAUDE.md?
465	- Cache strategy for the GitHub master: local clone refreshed
466	  on demand, or fetched once per session in the hook?
467	- What happens when the master moves forward but a project
468	  intentionally pins an older version? A `--pin` flag on
469	  `vc-x1 sync-claude-md`, or a version header line in the
470	  project copy?
471	- Interaction with the existing memory system (MEMORY.md
472	  index auto-loaded at session start): should any current
473	  memory content migrate to BOT-PROJECT.md, or is memory/
474	  strictly user-preference/ephemeral state that coexists?
475	- Should a pre-commit hook also remind the bot to re-read
476	  CLAUDE.md's commit sections, as a discipline backstop
477	  complementary to the drift check?
478	
479	### Not doing (yet)
480	
481	- Any implementation (hooks, `vc-x1` subcommands, the master
482	  GitHub repo, the import footer in CLAUDE.md).
483	- Any split of current CLAUDE.md content into general vs.
484	  project-local. That waits until the design is settled.
485	
486	### Next step
487	
488	Cogitate. When ready, pick a scope (bot-infra repo vs.
489	in-project) and open a plan.
490	
491	## Design: actor runtime + probe microbench system (0.8.0-dev0)
492	
493	Design discussion captured for later implementation; no code
494	changes in this step. Splits follow-on work across two repos —
```

638	## Plan: probe primitive + probe-mpsc-2t (0.8.0-dev1)
639	
640	Design-only capture of the `dev2` implementation plan for the
641	free-form `Probe` primitive and its first probed bench. No code
642	in this step.
643	
644	The dev0 preview listed a different cadence (dev1 = implement
645	probe primitive, dev2 = instrument existing benches). Reshuffled
646	to plan-then-implement to match dev0's pattern and give the
647	design a separate reviewable checkpoint. The old preview is
648	left stale; this section is the current truth.
649	
650	### Scope
651	
652	Introduce the minimum viable `Probe` primitive and validate it
653	by instrumenting `mpsc-2t`. Goal is to quantify probe overhead
654	via side-by-side comparison with the unprobed bench, not to get
655	a realistic "send cost" number on `std::sync::mpsc` — send
656	latency on that primitive (~15–30 ns) is comparable to probe
657	overhead (~20 ns per probe = one minstant `now()` pair plus
658	histogram record).
659	
660	That's intentional: dev2 validates the probe primitive under
661	worst-case signal-to-noise. A probe that produces coherent
662	numbers here will produce cleaner numbers on any heavier
663	workload.
664	
665	### Probe surface (minimal)
666	
667	    pub struct Probe { name: String, hist: Histogram<u64> }
668	    impl Probe {
669	        pub fn new(name: &str) -> Self
670	        pub fn record(&mut self, ns: u64)
671	        pub fn report(&self)  // own mini-formatter
672	    }
673	
674	- Owned, not shared. `&mut self` for `record`. No internal
675	  locking.
676	- `Send`: `Histogram<u64>` is `Send`, so probes can be moved
677	  across threads (e.g. returned from a `JoinHandle<Probe>`).
678	  Not `Sync` — cross-thread *sharing* is out of scope.
679	- Module location: `src/probe.rs`. Registered in `src/main.rs`
680	  with `mod probe;`.
681	
682	### Probed bench: `probe-mpsc-2t`
683	
684	Structural mirror of `mpsc-2t`, diverging only in
685	instrumentation:
686	
687	- File: `src/benches/probe_mpsc_2t.rs`
688	- CLI name: `probe-mpsc-2t` (prefix convention — all probed
689	  variants will be `probe-*`, keeping them alphabetically
690	  grouped in the startup listing).
691	- Struct: `ProbedStdMpsc2Thread`, mirrors `StdMpsc2Thread` plus:
692	  - `main_probe: Probe` field — records main thread's
693	    `req_tx.send(c)` duration inside `step()`.
694	  - Worker thread owns its own `Probe`, records around
695	    `resp_tx.send(v)`, returns it via `JoinHandle<Probe>` on
696	    shutdown.
697	- New method `finish(&mut self) -> (Probe, Probe)` — drops
698	  `req_tx` (worker `recv` returns `Err`, worker exits), joins
699	  the worker thread, returns `(main_probe, worker_probe)`.
700	  `Drop` impl stays as a safety net for panic paths.
701	- `pub fn run()` flow:
702	  1. `bench = new(...)`
703	  2. `(hist, outer, inner, duration_s) = run_adaptive(&mut bench, cfg)`
704	  3. `(main_probe, worker_probe) = bench.finish()`
705	  4. `harness::print_report(...)` — existing outer-histogram
706	     report.
707	  5. `main_probe.report()`
708	  6. `worker_probe.report()`
709	
710	### Probe report format
711	
712	Own formatter; not a reuse of `harness::print_report`. Reason:
713	`print_report` assumes outer/inner/adj-per-call semantics that
714	don't apply to a probe — each `record()` is one sample, period.
715	
716	MVP shape:
717	
718	    probe: <name> [count=N]
719	      <band-table: min-p1, p1-p10, ..., p99-max>
720	      mean:           ... ns
721	      stdev:          ... ns
722	      mean min-p99:   ... ns
723	      stdev min-p99:  ... ns
724	
725	Reuse the band-boundary percentages and column layout from
726	`harness::print_report` so rows line up visually with the outer
727	report. If the band-table renderer falls out naturally as a
728	shared helper during implementation, factor it; don't force it
729	if the shapes diverge.
730	
731	### Validation experiment
732	
733	Once dev2 merges, run both variants back-to-back with a large
734	budget and matched pinning:
735	
736	    iiac-perf mpsc-2t probe-mpsc-2t -D 60 --pin <core-a>,<core-b>
737	
738	Extract per-step probe overhead as:
739	
740	    overhead_per_step = mean(probe-mpsc-2t) - mean(mpsc-2t)
741	
742	Since `probe-mpsc-2t` adds exactly two probes per step (one on
743	each thread), divide by 2 for per-probe overhead. Expected
744	range: ~20–40 ns (2 × minstant `now()` + histogram record).
745	
746	Both variants run in the same process invocation so CPU state
747	and framing calibration are shared, minimizing drift between
748	the two measurements.
749	
750	### Intentionally out of scope for dev2
751	
752	- Scoped / RAII probe sugar (slated for a later dev).
753	- Probing `mpsc-1t` (can follow later if useful).
754	- Cross-thread *sharing* of a single probe (no use case yet).
755	- Message-carried `sent_at` timestamps / travel-time probes
756	  (wait for a bench that motivates them).
757	- Any modification to `harness` (probes stay bench-local).
758	- Probe overhead subtraction in the calibration model
759	  (dev3+ territory).
760	
761	### Next step
762	
763	Approval from user, then implement as `0.8.0-dev2`.
764	
765	## Implement probe primitive + probe-mpsc-2t (0.8.0-dev2)
766	
767	Implements the 0.8.0-dev1 plan: free-form `Probe` primitive and
```

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/notes/chores-03.md",
  "content": "# Chores-03\n\nContinuation of `chores-02.md`, which crossed 1,200 lines. Same\nformat; see [Chores format](README.md#chores-format).\n\n## Plan: TProbe start/end (0.9.0-dev1)\n\nPlanning commit for 0.9.0. No code change in this step. Introduces\na scope-based recording API on `TProbe` (`start` / `end`) that\nkeeps the hot path to a pair of tick reads plus a record append —\nall delta math and histogram ingestion deferred to report time.\n\nDesign sketch lives in\n[ideas.md — Tprobe](ideas.md#tprobe-time-probe). This plan fixes\nthe subset that will actually land across 0.9.0.\n\n### Scope\n\nAdd `TProbe::start` / `TProbe::end` as a second recording path on\nthe existing `TProbe`. The existing `record(ticks: u64)` stays\nfor now; it is not replaced until a follow-on step lands a full\nreplacement (scope-based recording + new report formatting).\n\n### Surface (to be implemented in dev2)\n\n```rust\n#[must_use]\n#[derive(Clone, Copy)]\npub struct TProbeRecId {\n    site_id: u64,\n    start_tsc: u64,\n}\n\nimpl TProbe {\n    pub fn start(&mut self, site_id: u64) -> TProbeRecId;\n    pub fn end(&mut self, tpri: TProbeRecId);\n}\n```\n\n- `start(site_id)` reads `ticks::read_ticks()` and returns a\n  `TProbeRecId` carrying `(site_id, start_tsc)`. No buffer\n  allocation — the id holds the start tick itself (Option B in\n  ideas.md).\n- `end(tpri)` reads `ticks::read_ticks()` and pushes a complete\n  record `(site_id, start_tsc, end_tsc)` onto the probe's record\n  buffer. Buffer only ever holds complete records.\n\n### Record buffer\n\n```rust\nstruct Record { site_id: u64, start_tsc: u64, end_tsc: u64 }\n```\n\n`Vec<Record>` field on `TProbe`, appended to at `end()` time.\nUnbounded for the first cut; bounded / ring-buffer policy\ndeferred.\n\n### Raw ticks, not deltas\n\nRecords store raw `start_tsc` + `end_tsc`, not `end − start`.\nTwo reasons:\n\n1. Skipping the subtraction keeps the hot path to `rdtscp` +\n   store, without an arithmetic op between the read and the\n   append.\n2. Raw TSC values retain record-order information across\n   interleaved scopes or sites — useful for correlating a\n   stream, which a delta-only layout loses.\n\nDelta = `end_tsc − start_tsc` is computed at report time.\n\n### site_id policy\n\n`site_id: u64` is supplied by the caller. For the first cut, the\nsimplest generator is a single global `AtomicU64` counter (users\ncache the returned id at each call site via `LazyLock` or a\n`static`). A per-`TProbe` counter is equivalent for uniqueness\nwithin one probe's scope — pick whichever first caller needs.\n\nAuto-generated site_ids from a compile-time const hash of\n`file!()` / `line!()`, macros, and an `inventory`/`linkme` slice\nfor name resolution stay as [ideas.md open\nwork](ideas.md#auto-generated-site_id-compile-time-zero-user-effort)\n— not in 0.9.0.\n\n### Coexistence with `record(ticks)`\n\n`TProbe::record(ticks)` is untouched and keeps feeding the\nexisting histogram directly. `start` / `end` populate the new\nrecord buffer. At report time, the buffer is drained into the\nsame histogram (delta per record), so `report()` renders one\ncombined band-table regardless of which path produced samples.\n\nRemoving `record(ticks)` waits until the scope API has a full\nreplacement (report reshape + bench migration).\n\n### Report processing (dev3)\n\nFirst cut: lazy — `report()` drains the record buffer into the\nexisting histogram on first call (delta = `end_tsc − start_tsc`,\nclamped to `1` to respect the histogram lower bound), then prints\nthe same band-table as today. `site_id` is ignored in this pass.\n\nPer-site sub-tables (one band-table per distinct `site_id`) is a\nfollow-on once more than one site is in use.\n\n### Hot-path cost\n\nTarget: `start` and `end` each compile to roughly one\n`read_ticks()` + register moves, plus — for `end` — one `Vec`\npush. No branches on the hot path beyond what `Vec::push` itself\nneeds (capacity check). The deferred-math choice is what buys\nthis.\n\n### Edits (this dev1 commit)\n\n- `Cargo.toml` — version bump `0.8.0` → `0.9.0-dev1`.\n- `notes/todo.md` — In Progress entry + dev1 Done entry +\n  reference `[28]`.\n- `notes/chores-03.md` — new file; this section.\n\n### Intentionally out of scope\n\nEverything below is deferred:\n\n- Double-end detection (extra branch vs. silent bug class) —\n  revisit alongside the dev2 buffer impl.\n- Guard / auto-scope sugar on top of `start` / `end`.\n- Ring-buffer / bounded policy for long-running recording.\n- Userinfo payload on records.\n- Per-site grouping in the report.\n- Auto-generated `site_id` via const hash + macro + inventory\n  slice.\n- Replacing `record(ticks)`.\n\n### Next step\n\nApproval from user, then implement as `0.9.0-dev2` (types +\n`start` / `end` + record buffer).\n\n### Preview of remaining steps\n\n- `dev2` — implement types + `start` / `end` + record buffer\n  (no report wiring yet; unit tests for start/end round-trip).\n- `dev3` — lazy report ingestion: `report()` drains records into\n  the histogram.\n- `dev4` — wire `start` / `end` into an existing bench (likely\n  `tp-pc`) as the first consumer, validate numbers.\n- `0.9.0` final — remove `-devN`, roll the devs up, move entries\n  to `## Done`.\n"
}
```

1	# Chores-03
2	
3	Continuation of `chores-02.md`, which crossed 1,200 lines. Same
4	format; see [Chores format](README.md#chores-format).
5	
6	## Plan: TProbe start/end (0.9.0-dev1)
7	
8	Planning commit for 0.9.0. No code change in this step. Introduces
9	a scope-based recording API on `TProbe` (`start` / `end`) that
10	keeps the hot path to a pair of tick reads plus a record append —
11	all delta math and histogram ingestion deferred to report time.
12	
13	Design sketch lives in
14	[ideas.md — Tprobe](ideas.md#tprobe-time-probe). This plan fixes
15	the subset that will actually land across 0.9.0.
16	
17	### Scope
18	
19	Add `TProbe::start` / `TProbe::end` as a second recording path on
20	the existing `TProbe`. The existing `record(ticks: u64)` stays
21	for now; it is not replaced until a follow-on step lands a full
22	replacement (scope-based recording + new report formatting).
23	
24	### Surface (to be implemented in dev2)
25	
26	```rust
27	#[must_use]
28	#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
29	pub struct TProbeRecId {
30	    site_id: u64,
31	    start_tsc: u64,
32	}
33	
34	impl TProbe {
35	    pub fn start(&mut self, site_id: u64) -> TProbeRecId;
36	    pub fn end(&mut self, tpri: TProbeRecId);
37	}
38	```
39	
40	- `start(site_id)` reads `ticks::read_ticks()` and returns a
41	  `TProbeRecId` carrying `(site_id, start_tsc)`. No buffer
42	  allocation — the id holds the start tick itself (Option B in
43	  ideas.md).
44	- `end(tpri)` reads `ticks::read_ticks()` and pushes a complete
45	  record `(site_id, start_tsc, end_tsc)` onto the probe's record
46	  buffer. Buffer only ever holds complete records.
47	
48	### Record buffer
49	
50	```rust
51	struct Record { site_id: u64, start_tsc: u64, end_tsc: u64 }
52	```
53	
54	`Vec<Record>` field on `TProbe`, appended to at `end()` time.
55	Unbounded for the first cut; bounded / ring-buffer policy
56	deferred.
57	
58	### Raw ticks, not deltas
59	
60	Records store raw `start_tsc` + `end_tsc`, not `end − start`.
61	Two reasons:
62	
63	1. Skipping the subtraction keeps the hot path to `rdtscp` +
64	   store, without an arithmetic op between the read and the
65	   append.
66	2. Raw TSC values retain record-order information across
67	   interleaved scopes or sites — useful for correlating a
68	   stream, which a delta-only layout loses.
69	
70	Delta = `end_tsc − start_tsc` is computed at report time.
71	
72	### site_id policy
73	
74	`site_id: u64` is supplied by the caller. For the first cut, the
75	simplest generator is a single global `AtomicU64` counter (users
76	cache the returned id at each call site via `LazyLock` or a
77	`static`). A per-`TProbe` counter is equivalent for uniqueness
78	within one probe's scope — pick whichever first caller needs.
79	
80	Auto-generated site_ids from a compile-time const hash of
81	`file!()` / `line!()`, macros, and an `inventory`/`linkme` slice
82	for name resolution stay as [ideas.md open
83	work](ideas.md#auto-generated-site_id-compile-time-zero-user-effort)
84	— not in 0.9.0.
85	
86	### Coexistence with `record(ticks)`
87	
88	`TProbe::record(ticks)` is untouched and keeps feeding the
89	existing histogram directly. `start` / `end` populate the new
90	record buffer. At report time, the buffer is drained into the
91	same histogram (delta per record), so `report()` renders one
92	combined band-table regardless of which path produced samples.
93	
94	Removing `record(ticks)` waits until the scope API has a full
95	replacement (report reshape + bench migration).
96	
97	### Report processing (dev3)
98	
99	First cut: lazy — `report()` drains the record buffer into the
100	existing histogram on first call (delta = `end_tsc − start_tsc`,
101	clamped to `1` to respect the histogram lower bound), then prints
102	the same band-table as today. `site_id` is ignored in this pass.
103	
104	Per-site sub-tables (one band-table per distinct `site_id`) is a
105	follow-on once more than one site is in use.
106	
107	### Hot-path cost
108	
109	Target: `start` and `end` each compile to roughly one
110	`read_ticks()` + register moves, plus — for `end` — one `Vec`
111	push. No branches on the hot path beyond what `Vec::push` itself
112	needs (capacity check). The deferred-math choice is what buys
113	this.
114	
115	### Edits (this dev1 commit)
116	
117	- `Cargo.toml` — version bump `0.8.0` → `0.9.0-dev1`.
118	- `notes/todo.md` — In Progress entry + dev1 Done entry +
119	  reference `[28]`.
120	- `notes/chores-03.md` — new file; this section.
121	
122	### Intentionally out of scope
123	
124	Everything below is deferred:
125	
126	- Double-end detection (extra branch vs. silent bug class) —
127	  revisit alongside the dev2 buffer impl.
128	- Guard / auto-scope sugar on top of `start` / `end`.
129	- Ring-buffer / bounded policy for long-running recording.
130	- Userinfo payload on records.
131	- Per-site grouping in the report.
132	- Auto-generated `site_id` via const hash + macro + inventory
133	  slice.
134	- Replacing `record(ticks)`.
135	
136	### Next step
137	
138	Approval from user, then implement as `0.9.0-dev2` (types +
139	`start` / `end` + record buffer).
140	
141	### Preview of remaining steps
142	
143	- `dev2` — implement types + `start` / `end` + record buffer
144	  (no report wiring yet; unit tests for start/end round-trip).
145	- `dev3` — lazy report ingestion: `report()` drains records into
146	  the histogram.
147	- `dev4` — wire `start` / `end` into an existing bench (likely
148	  `tp-pc`) as the first consumer, validate numbers.
149	- `0.9.0` final — remove `-devN`, roll the devs up, move entries
150	  to `## Done`.
151	
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/tprobe.rs",
  "old_string": "/// A named, single-writer histogram of hardware tick-counter\n/// deltas. Not `Sync`; cross-thread *sharing* is out of scope.\n/// `Send` so probes can be moved between threads (e.g. returned\n/// via a `JoinHandle<TProbe>` on shutdown).\npub struct TProbe {\n    name: String,\n    hist: Histogram<u64>,\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Opaque handle returned by [`TProbe::start`], consumed by\n/// [`TProbe::end`]. Carries the caller-supplied `site_id` and\n/// the start-time tick reading; no probe-internal allocation\n/// happens at `start` time.\n///\n/// `#[must_use]` — dropping the id without passing it to\n/// [`TProbe::end`] leaks the scope (no record is appended).\n#[must_use]\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]\npub struct TProbeRecId {\n    site_id: u64,\n    start_tsc: u64,\n}\n\n/// A complete scope record: `(site_id, start_tsc, end_tsc)`.\n/// Appended at [`TProbe::end`] time; the record buffer only\n/// ever holds complete records. Delta and histogram ingestion\n/// are deferred to [`TProbe::report`].\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]\nstruct Record {\n    site_id: u64,\n    start_tsc: u64,\n    end_tsc: u64,\n}\n\n/// A named, single-writer histogram of hardware tick-counter\n/// deltas plus a scope-record buffer. Not `Sync`; cross-thread\n/// *sharing* is out of scope. `Send` so probes can be moved\n/// between threads (e.g. returned via a `JoinHandle<TProbe>`\n/// on shutdown).\npub struct TProbe {\n    name: String,\n    hist: Histogram<u64>,\n    records: Vec<Record>,\n}"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/tprobe.rs",
  "old_string": "        Self {\n            name: name.to_string(),\n            hist: Histogram::<u64>::new_with_bounds(1, 1_000_000_000_000, 3).unwrap(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Record a single sample, in tick-counter deltas. Values\n    /// of 0 are clamped to 1 since the histogram's lower bound\n    /// is 1; back-to-back tick reads can produce 0 on fast cores.\n    pub fn record(&mut self, ticks: u64) {\n        self.hist.record(ticks.max(1)).unwrap();\n    }",
  "new_string": "        Self {\n            name: name.to_string(),\n            hist: Histogram::<u64>::new_with_bounds(1, 1_000_000_000_000, 3).unwrap(),\n            records: Vec::new(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Record a single sample, in tick-counter deltas. Values\n    /// of 0 are clamped to 1 since the histogram's lower bound\n    /// is 1; back-to-back tick reads can produce 0 on fast cores.\n    pub fn record(&mut self, ticks: u64) {\n        self.hist.record(ticks.max(1)).unwrap();\n    }\n\n    /// Begin a scope. Reads the hardware tick counter and\n    /// returns an opaque [`TProbeRecId`] carrying `(site_id,\n    /// start_tsc)`. The id must eventually be passed to\n    /// [`TProbe::end`]; a dropped id leaves no record.\n    #[inline]\n    pub fn start(&mut self, site_id: u64) -> TProbeRecId {\n        TProbeRecId {\n            site_id,\n            start_tsc: ticks::read_ticks(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// End the scope started by [`TProbe::start`]. Reads the\n    /// hardware tick counter and appends a complete record\n    /// `(site_id, start_tsc, end_tsc)` to the probe's record\n    /// buffer. Delta and histogram ingestion are deferred to\n    /// [`TProbe::report`].\n    #[inline]\n    pub fn end(&mut self, tpri: TProbeRecId) {\n        let end_tsc = ticks::read_ticks();\n        self.records.push(Record {\n            site_id: tpri.site_id,\n            start_tsc: tpri.start_tsc,\n            end_tsc,\n        });\n    }"
}

}
