  "old_string": "### Preview of remaining steps\n\n- `dev2` — implement types + `start` / `end` + record buffer\n  (no report wiring yet; unit tests for start/end round-trip).\n- `dev3` — lazy report ingestion: `report()` drains records into\n  the histogram.\n- `dev4` — wire `start` / `end` into an existing bench (likely\n  `tp-pc`) as the first consumer, validate numbers.\n- `0.9.0` final — remove `-devN`, roll the devs up, move entries\n  to `## Done`.",
  "new_string": "### Preview of remaining steps\n\n- `dev2` — implement types + `start` / `end` + record buffer\n  (no report wiring yet; unit tests for start/end round-trip).\n- `dev3` — lazy report ingestion: `report()` drains records into\n  the histogram.\n- `dev4` — wire `start` / `end` into an existing bench (likely\n  `tp-pc`) as the first consumer, validate numbers.\n- `0.9.0` final — remove `-devN`, roll the devs up, move entries\n  to `## Done`.\n\n## Implement TProbe start/end + buffer (0.9.0-dev2)\n\nImplements the 0.9.0-dev1 plan's surface: `TProbeRecId`,\n`TProbe::start` / `end`, and a `records: Vec<Record>` buffer on\nthe probe. Report wiring is not part of this step; `record(ticks)`\nand the histogram path are unchanged. Verified via four new unit\ntests and a smoke-test run of `tp-pc` (output unchanged).\n\n### Edits\n\n- `Cargo.toml` — version bump to `0.9.0-dev2`.\n- `src/tprobe.rs`:\n  - Added `pub struct TProbeRecId { site_id: u64, start_tsc: u64 }`\n    with `#[must_use]`, `#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]` and\n    private fields.\n  - Added internal `struct Record { site_id, start_tsc, end_tsc }`.\n  - Added `records: Vec<Record>` field on `TProbe`; initialized\n    empty in `TProbe::new`.\n  - Added `#[inline] pub fn start(&mut self, site_id: u64)\n    -> TProbeRecId`.\n  - Added `#[inline] pub fn end(&mut self, tpri: TProbeRecId)`.\n  - Added `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` with four tests:\n    `start_end_appends_one_record`,\n    `start_end_preserves_start_tsc`,\n    `start_end_interleaved_non_stack` (non-stack nesting —\n    `start(a) start(b) end(a) end(b)` — succeeds per Option B),\n    `record_and_start_end_are_independent` (histogram and record\n    buffer are unaffected by each other).\n- `notes/todo.md` — dev2 Done entry + reference `[29]`.\n- `notes/chores-03.md` — this section.\n\n### Findings\n\n- **Binary-crate dead-code warnings.** `clippy -D warnings`\n  flags the new pub items (`TProbe::start`, `TProbe::end`, the\n  `Record` struct, the `records` field) as dead code because\n  `iiac-perf` is a binary crate and nothing in `main.rs` reaches\n  them yet. Tests under `#[cfg(test)]` don't count for the\n  non-test dead-code analysis. Added `#[allow(dead_code)]` to\n  each of the four items with a one-line note pointing at the\n  dev that will remove the allow (dev3 drains `records` +\n  `Record` in `report()`; dev4 wires `start`/`end` into a\n  bench). The allows will be stripped in those devs.\n- **`&mut self` on `start`.** `start` doesn't strictly need\n  `&mut self` — the hot path is `ticks::read_ticks()` +\n  moves. Kept `&mut self` to match `record(&mut self, ...)`\n  and to leave room for future hot-path state without an API\n  break. Since `TProbeRecId` is `Copy`, the exclusive borrow\n  is released at the `let id = probe.start(...)` return; the\n  later `probe.end(id)` borrow is independent.\n- **Monotonic assertion.** The round-trip test asserts\n  `end_tsc >= start_tsc`. On invariant TSC with a single-core\n  scope this always holds; if a future test migrates scopes\n  across cores it may need relaxing.\n- **Smoke test.** `iiac-perf tp-pc -d 0.1` (3900X, idle) —\n  numeric output matches dev5 within run-to-run variance;\n  no regression from adding the buffer field + new methods."
}

```
1	//! x86_64 impl of the tick-counter abstraction: `rdtsc` for
2	//! reads, CPUID-based invariant-TSC detection, and a 10 ms
3	//! spin-loop calibration for ticks-per-nanosecond.
4	
5	use std::sync::OnceLock;
6	use std::time::Duration;
7	
8	#[inline(always)]
9	pub fn read_ticks() -> u64 {
10	    // Safe on any x86_64 CPU: TSC has been present since the
11	    // original Pentium.
12	    unsafe { core::arch::x86_64::_rdtsc() }
13	}
14	
15	static TICKS_PER_NS: OnceLock<f64> = OnceLock::new();
16	
17	pub fn ticks_per_ns() -> f64 {
18	    *TICKS_PER_NS.get_or_init(calibrate)
19	}
20	
21	/// Spin for ~10 ms while reading `minstant::Instant` elapsed ns
22	/// and raw `rdtsc` ticks at each end. `minstant` computes its
23	/// own nanos-per-cycle internally but doesn't expose it, so we
24	/// rederive the ratio from the two independent measurements.
25	fn calibrate() -> f64 {
26	    let start_instant = minstant::Instant::now();
27	    let start_tsc = read_ticks();
28	    let target = Duration::from_millis(10);
29	    loop {
30	        let elapsed = start_instant.elapsed();
31	        if elapsed >= target {
32	            let end_tsc = read_ticks();
33	            let dtk = end_tsc.wrapping_sub(start_tsc) as f64;
34	            let dns = elapsed.as_nanos() as f64;
35	            return dtk / dns;
36	        }
37	        core::hint::spin_loop();
38	    }
39	}
40	
41	pub fn require_ok() {
42	    if !has_invariant_tsc() {
43	        eprintln!(
44	            "error: invariant TSC not supported by this CPU \
45	             (CPUID.80000007h:EDX[bit 8] = 0). iiac-perf probes \
46	             require a fixed-rate, non-stopping TSC; refusing to run."
47	        );
48	        std::process::exit(1);
49	    }
50	    if !minstant::is_tsc_available() {
51	        eprintln!(
52	            "error: TSC not selected as a kernel clocksource. \
53	             The CPU advertises invariant TSC, but the kernel has \
54	             rejected it — likely a sync or drift issue. iiac-perf \
55	             won't use a clock source the kernel considers unreliable; \
56	             refusing to run."
57	        );
58	        std::process::exit(1);
59	    }
60	}
61	
62	/// `CPUID.80000007h:EDX[bit 8]` — invariant TSC. Set iff the
63	/// TSC runs at a constant rate regardless of P-state changes
64	/// and keeps ticking in deep C-states. Both Intel and AMD
65	/// expose the feature at this bit.
66	fn has_invariant_tsc() -> bool {
67	    use core::arch::x86_64::__cpuid;
68	    let max_ext = __cpuid(0x8000_0000).eax;
69	    if max_ext < 0x8000_0007 {
70	        return false;
71	    }
72	    let leaf = __cpuid(0x8000_0007);
73	    (leaf.edx >> 8) & 1 == 1
74	}
75	
```

> AGENT

Both answered:

**1. Release tests — yes.** `cargo test --release` just ran: all 12 pass in release. Worth considering as a permanent addition to the pre-commit checklist for hot-path code; release is where inlining and OoO reordering actually happen. Up to you whether to amend the checklist now or leave it for a later CLAUDE.md tweak.

**2. Likelihood `a.start_tsc == b.start_tsc` — effectively zero, and the test doesn't depend on it anyway.**

- `read_ticks()` uses `_rdtsc()` on x86_64 (`src/ticks/x86_64.rs:12`). TSC advances every reference cycle on invariant-TSC hardware.
- Between the two `p.start(...)` calls there are multiple instructions (rdtsc → struct build → return → assign → prologue → rdtsc); even with aggressive inlining/OoO, back-to-back rdtsc reads on the same core typically differ by 20–30+ cycles. Modern CPUs effectively never produce identical reads here.
- Even if they did collide, the test would still pass: the assertions are `records.len() == 2`, `records[0].site_id == 1`, `records[1].site_id == 2`. Nothing asserts `a.start_tsc != b.start_tsc`.
- Design is robust to collision by construction — `TProbeRecId` is opaque data the caller hands back at `end()`. There's no lookup, no "which scope am I closing?" matching. Two scopes with identical `(site_id, start_tsc)` just produce two records with identical first two fields; that's a data-fidelity concern (you can't tell the records apart in the buffer), not a correctness concern.

If you want the test to *prove* non-stack ordering independent of tsc values, the site_id assertion already does that — the fact that `records[0].site_id == 1` and `records[1].site_id == 2` despite `end(a)` being called while `b` was still open is exactly the non-stack case.

  "new_string": "- **Smoke test.** `iiac-perf tp-pc -d 0.1` (3900X, idle) —\n  numeric output matches dev5 within run-to-run variance;\n  no regression from adding the buffer field + new methods.\n- **`start_tsc` uniqueness is not an invariant.** `TProbeRecId`\n  is opaque data the caller hands back at `end()`; there is no\n  lookup or \"which scope am I closing?\" matching — `end()` just\n  appends whatever fields the id carries. Two scopes that\n  happen to share `(site_id, start_tsc)` produce two\n  indistinguishable records, which is a data-fidelity concern,\n  not a correctness one. Keeping uniqueness out of the\n  invariant set is what lets `start`/`end` stay at \"one tick\n  read plus record-keeping\" — adding dedup would cost more\n  than it saves. In practice the stack return trip around\n  `start` is comfortably more than one TSC tick, so collisions\n  don't occur on current hardware anyway.\n- **Release-mode tests run clean.** `cargo test --release`\n  passes all 12 (see CLAUDE.md pre-commit checklist step 4 —\n  added this pass)."
}

  "new_string": "    /// Render a band-table report for this probe. `as_ticks`\n    /// controls the display unit: `false` converts stored tick\n    /// deltas to nanoseconds (default for the CLI); `true` shows\n    /// raw ticks (`-t`/`--ticks`).\n    ///\n    /// Drains any pending `start`/`end` records into the histogram\n    /// before rendering: `delta = end_tsc − start_tsc`, clamped to\n    /// `1` since the histogram lower bound is 1.\n    pub fn report(&mut self, as_ticks: bool) {\n        for r in self.records.drain(..) {\n            let delta = r.end_tsc.saturating_sub(r.start_tsc);\n            self.hist.record(delta.max(1)).unwrap();\n        }\n        let sample_count = self.hist.len();"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "    #[test]\n    fn record_and_start_end_are_independent() {\n        let mut p = TProbe::new(\"t\");\n        let id = p.start(1);\n        p.end(id);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 0);\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 1);\n\n        p.record(100);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 1);\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 1);\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "    #[test]\n    fn record_and_start_end_are_independent() {\n        let mut p = TProbe::new(\"t\");\n        let id = p.start(1);\n        p.end(id);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 0);\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 1);\n\n        p.record(100);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 1);\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 1);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn report_drains_records_into_histogram() {\n        let mut p = TProbe::new(\"t\");\n        let id1 = p.start(1);\n        p.end(id1);\n        let id2 = p.start(2);\n        p.end(id2);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 0);\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 2);\n\n        p.report(false);\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 0);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 2);\n\n        // Idempotent: a second report drains nothing, hist unchanged.\n        p.report(false);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 2);\n    }\n}"
}

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/notes/chores-03.md",
  "old_string": "- **Release-mode tests run clean.** `cargo test --release`\n  passes all 12 (see CLAUDE.md pre-commit checklist step 4 —\n  added this pass).",
  "new_string": "- **Release-mode tests run clean.** `cargo test --release`\n  passes all 12 (see CLAUDE.md pre-commit checklist step 4 —\n  added this pass).\n\n## Lazy report drain: records → histogram (0.9.0-dev3)\n\nWires the `records` buffer into `TProbe::report`: on each call,\npending `(start_tsc, end_tsc)` pairs are drained and converted to\ntick-delta samples in the histogram before the band-table is\nrendered. Completes the scope-API path — `start` → `end` →\neventually visible in a `report()` — while keeping the existing\n`record(ticks)` path untouched.\n\n### Edits\n\n- `Cargo.toml` — version bump to `0.9.0-dev3`.\n- `src/tprobe.rs`:\n  - `report(&self, ...)` → `report(&mut self, ...)`.\n  - Drain loop at top of `report`: for each `Record`,\n    `hist.record((end_tsc.saturating_sub(start_tsc)).max(1))`.\n  - Removed `#[allow(dead_code)]` on `Record` and on the\n    `records` field (both are live now).\n  - Left `#[allow(dead_code)]` on `site_id` (read once per-site\n    grouping lands; see out-of-scope list) and on the\n    `start` / `end` methods (first non-test caller lands in\n    dev4).\n  - Added unit test `report_drains_records_into_histogram`:\n    start/end twice → hist empty, records = 2 → report →\n    records = 0, hist = 2; second report is a no-op.\n- `src/benches/tp_pc.rs` — `let producer_probe = …` /\n  `let consumer_probe = …` → `let mut …` to satisfy the new\n  `&mut self` on `report`. `tp-pc` still populates via\n  `record(delta)` manually, so the `records` buffer stays\n  empty and the drain is a no-op; output shape unchanged.\n- `notes/todo.md` — dev3 Done entry + reference `[30]`.\n- `notes/chores-03.md` — this section.\n\n### Findings\n\n- **Drain placement.** Drain runs at the very top of `report`,\n  before `sample_count` is computed, so a probe that only used\n  `start`/`end` (empty `hist` but non-empty `records`)\n  produces the full band-table rather than the\n  `sample_count == 0` short-circuit.\n- **Delta clamp.** Uses `saturating_sub(..).max(1)` to match the\n  existing `record(ticks)` behavior. Back-to-back tick reads on\n  a fast core can legitimately produce 0, which the histogram\n  rejects (lower bound 1). `saturating_sub` guards against\n  any pathological `end < start` case (e.g. a scope that crossed\n  cores without invariant-TSC coherence) — would rarely matter,\n  but cheaper than a panic path.\n- **Idempotent report.** `records.drain(..)` empties the vec,\n  so a second `report()` has nothing to drain and just\n  re-renders the histogram state. Useful: `tp-pc` calls\n  `report` twice (once per probe), each on a distinct probe,\n  but a pattern that reports the same probe twice still works.\n- **`site_id` dead for now.** The drain path ignores `site_id`\n  (all samples lumped into the single histogram). Kept the\n  field populated so per-site grouping in a later step is a\n  drop-in without changing record shape. `#[allow(dead_code)]`\n  scoped to just that field.\n- **`&mut self` ripple.** Only `tp-pc` called `TProbe::report`;\n  changing the receiver required `let mut …` on two bindings\n  there. No other callers.\n- **Smoke test.** `iiac-perf tp-pc -d 0.1` — consumer loop\n  mean min-p99 ≈ 9,091 ns; producer loop similar. Matches\n  dev2 range within normal variance."
}
```

```
1	//! TProbe variant of `producer-consumer`: a dedicated producer
2	//! thread and a dedicated consumer thread trade messages over
3	//! two `std::sync::mpsc` channels. Each actor measures its own
4	//! full loop iteration via a [`TProbe`], reading hardware tick
5	//! deltas directly through [`crate::ticks::read_ticks`] instead
6	//! of going through `minstant::Instant::now()` +
7	//! `elapsed().as_nanos()`.
8	//!
9	//! Run back-to-back with `producer-consumer` to see whether
10	//! dropping the tick→ns conversion trims the per-sample framing.
11	
12	use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicBool, Ordering};
13	use std::sync::{Arc, mpsc};
14	use std::thread;
15	use std::time::Duration;
16	
17	use crate::harness::RunCfg;
18	use crate::pin;
19	use crate::ticks;
20	use crate::tprobe::TProbe;
21	
22	/// Registry name used on the CLI.
23	pub const NAME: &str = "tp-pc";
24	
25	/// Registry entry point.
26	pub fn run(cfg: &RunCfg) {
27	    let (req_tx, req_rx) = mpsc::channel::<u64>();
28	    let (resp_tx, resp_rx) = mpsc::channel::<u64>();
29	    let shutdown = Arc::new(AtomicBool::new(false));
30	
31	    let producer_cpu = cfg.core_for(0);
32	    let consumer_cpu = cfg.core_for(1);
33	
34	    let producer_shutdown = shutdown.clone();
35	    let producer = thread::spawn(move || {
36	        pin::pin_current(producer_cpu);
37	        let mut probe = TProbe::new("producer loop");
38	        let mut counter: u64 = 0;
39	        while !producer_shutdown.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {
40	            let s = ticks::read_ticks();
41	            counter = counter.wrapping_add(1);
42	            if req_tx.send(counter).is_err() {
43	                break;
44	            }
45	            if resp_rx.recv().is_err() {
46	                break;
47	            }
48	            let e = ticks::read_ticks();
49	            probe.record(e.wrapping_sub(s));
50	        }
51	        probe
52	    });
53	
54	    let consumer = thread::spawn(move || {
55	        pin::pin_current(consumer_cpu);
56	        let mut probe = TProbe::new("consumer loop");
57	        loop {
58	            let s = ticks::read_ticks();
59	            let v = match req_rx.recv() {
60	                Ok(v) => v,
61	                Err(_) => break,
62	            };
63	            if resp_tx.send(v).is_err() {
64	                break;
65	            }
66	            let e = ticks::read_ticks();
67	            probe.record(e.wrapping_sub(s));
68	        }
69	        probe
70	    });
71	
72	    thread::sleep(Duration::from_secs_f64(cfg.target_seconds));
73	    shutdown.store(true, Ordering::Relaxed);
74	
75	    let mut producer_probe = producer.join().expect("producer panicked");
76	    let mut consumer_probe = consumer.join().expect("consumer panicked");
77	
78	    println!(
79	        "tp-pc (2 threads, TProbe tick-only) [duration={:.1}s]:",
80	        cfg.target_seconds
81	    );
82	    producer_probe.report(cfg.report_ticks);
83	    consumer_probe.report(cfg.report_ticks);
84	}
85	
```

Run back-to-back with `producer-consumer` to see whether\n//! dropping the tick→ns conversion trims the per-sample framing.\n\nuse std::sync::atomic::{AtomicBool, Ordering};\nuse std::sync::{Arc, mpsc};\nuse std::thread;\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\nuse crate::harness::RunCfg;\nuse crate::pin;\nuse crate::ticks;\nuse crate::tprobe::TProbe;",

Each actor measures its own\n//! full loop iteration via [`TProbe::start`] / [`TProbe::end`],\n//! which read hardware ticks directly and defer delta / histogram\n//! ingestion to `report()` — skipping the tick→ns conversion on\n//! the hot path.\n//!\n//! Run back-to-back with `producer-consumer` to see whether\n//! dropping the tick→ns conversion trims the per-sample framing.\n\nuse std::sync::atomic::{AtomicBool, Ordering};\nuse std::sync::{Arc, mpsc};\nuse std::thread;\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\nuse crate::harness::RunCfg;\nuse crate::pin;\nuse crate::tprobe::TProbe;"

  "new_string": "    let producer_shutdown = shutdown.clone();\n    let producer = thread::spawn(move || {\n        pin::pin_current(producer_cpu);\n        let mut probe = TProbe::new(\"producer loop\");\n        let mut counter: u64 = 0;\n        while !producer_shutdown.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {\n            let id = probe.start(0);\n            counter = counter.wrapping_add(1);\n            if req_tx.send(counter).is_err() {\n                break;\n            }\n            if resp_rx.recv().is_err() {\n                break;\n            }\n            probe.end(id);\n        }\n        probe\n    });\n\n    let consumer = thread::spawn(move || {\n        pin::pin_current(consumer_cpu);\n        let mut probe = TProbe::new(\"consumer loop\");\n        loop {\n            let id = probe.start(0);\n            let v = match req_rx.recv() {\n                Ok(v) => v,\n                Err(_) => break,\n            };\n            if resp_tx.send(v).is_err() {\n                break;\n            }\n            probe.end(id);\n        }\n        probe\n    });"
}

  "old_string": "    /// Record a single sample, in tick-counter deltas. Values\n    /// of 0 are clamped to 1 since the histogram's lower bound\n    /// is 1; back-to-back tick reads can produce 0 on fast cores.\n    pub fn record(&mut self, ticks: u64) {\n        self.hist.record(ticks.max(1)).unwrap();\n    }",

Values\n    /// of 0 are clamped to 1 since the histogram's lower bound\n    /// is 1; back-to-back tick reads can produce 0 on fast cores.\n    ///\n    /// Held pending a decision on whether to remove in favor of\n    /// the scope API (`start` / `end`) — see\n    /// `notes/chores-03.md#intentionally-out-of-scope`.\n    #[allow(dead_code)]\n    pub fn record(&mut self, ticks: u64) {\n        self.hist.record(ticks.max(1)).unwrap();\n    }"
}

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/notes/chores-03.md",
  "old_string": "- **Smoke test.** `iiac-perf tp-pc -d 0.1` — consumer loop\n  mean min-p99 ≈ 9,091 ns; producer loop similar. Matches\n  dev2 range within normal variance.",
  "new_string": "- **Smoke test.** `iiac-perf tp-pc -d 0.1` — consumer loop\n  mean min-p99 ≈ 9,091 ns; producer loop similar. Matches\n  dev2 range within normal variance.\n\n## Wire tp-pc to TProbe start/end (0.9.0-dev4)\n\nFirst non-test consumer of the scope API. Replaces `tp-pc`'s\nmanual `ticks::read_ticks()` pair + `probe.record(delta)` with\n`probe.start(0)` / `probe.end(id)`. With this step, the full\nscope-API path — `start` → `end` → records buffer → drain in\n`report()` — is exercised end-to-end by a real bench. Removes\nthe dev2-era `#[allow(dead_code)]` on `TProbe::start` and\n`TProbe::end`. `TProbe::record` is no longer called from any\nbench and gets its own `#[allow(dead_code)]` pending the\nout-of-scope \"replace record(ticks)\" decision.\n\n### Edits\n\n- `Cargo.toml` — version bump to `0.9.0-dev4`.\n- `src/tprobe.rs`:\n  - Dropped `#[allow(dead_code)]` on `start` and `end`\n    (first non-test caller now exists).\n  - Added `#[allow(dead_code)]` on `record(ticks)` with a note\n    pointing at the out-of-scope removal decision — no bench\n    calls it anymore.\n- `src/benches/tp_pc.rs`:\n  - Producer loop: `let s = ticks::read_ticks(); … let e =\n    ticks::read_ticks(); probe.record(e.wrapping_sub(s));`\n    → `let id = probe.start(0); … probe.end(id);`.\n  - Consumer loop: same rewrite.\n  - Dropped the now-unused `use crate::ticks;`.\n  - Updated the `//!` module doc to reference the scope API\n    instead of `ticks::read_ticks`.\n- `notes/todo.md` — dev4 Done entry + reference `[31]`.\n- `notes/chores-03.md` — this section.\n\n### Findings\n\n- **A/B vs. dev3: dev4 runs hotter.** `iiac-perf tp-pc -d 0.5`\n  on the 3900X (idle, `--pin` default):\n\n  | version | producer mean min-p99 | consumer mean min-p99 |\n  |--------:|----------------------:|----------------------:|\n  | dev3    | ≈ 9,091 ns            | ≈ 9,091 ns            |\n  | dev4    | ≈ 7,625 ns            | ≈ 7,650 ns            |\n\n  Likely explanation: between-iteration overhead dropped from\n  `hist.record(delta)` (bucketization + atomic increment) to a\n  single `Vec::push`, which shortens the time both threads\n  spend on probe bookkeeping. The consumer stays hotter — data\n  is ready when the producer's `recv` arrives, fewer cold\n  wake-ups on the channel — and the whole round-trip settles\n  into a tighter regime. Net: dev4 numbers reflect the\n  round-trip cost in a hotter steady state rather than the\n  round-trip + probe-record mix.\n\n  Interpretation note: dev4 numbers are *not* directly\n  comparable to dev3 as \"TProbe got faster.\" They reflect a\n  different operating point (less between-iteration overhead\n  gives a hotter loop). If the goal is apples-to-apples\n  round-trip cost across versions, we'd need to pre-reserve\n  buffer capacity in dev4 (avoid reallocations) and compare\n  against a dev3 run of matched duration.\n- **Low-tail samples appearing in dev4.** `min-p1` now starts\n  as low as ~20 ns (producer) / ~130 ns (consumer) vs. dev3's\n  ~3,000 ns. Consistent with the \"hotter steady state\"\n  explanation — when the channel has data already buffered,\n  `recv` short-circuits and the round-trip can complete in a\n  few hundred cycles.\n- **Leaked ids on shutdown paths.** Both producer and\n  consumer loops start a scope, do channel I/O that may break\n  on `Err`, and only call `end(id)` on the happy path. On\n  break paths the id is dropped without matching `end`, which\n  per design just means the in-flight sample is lost (no\n  buffer reservation to clean up, no panic). Consistent with\n  the Option-B invariant that the buffer only ever holds\n  complete records.\n- **Hot-path cost is now *lower* than `record(ticks)`.** The\n  path used to be `read_ticks()` + `wrapping_sub` +\n  `probe.record()` (which calls `hist.record` — bucket math +\n  atomic). Now it's `read_ticks()` + `Vec::push`. The push is\n  O(1) amortized; reallocation shows up at report time as one\n  long-tail spike in the band table's `p99-max` row — still\n  the hot path is cheaper on average.\n- **Tests green in both debug and release.** All 13 still\n  pass (`cargo test` and `cargo test --release`)."
}

> AGENT

Summary of 0.9.0-dev4:

- `Cargo.toml`: `0.9.0-dev3` → `0.9.0-dev4`
- `src/tprobe.rs`:
  - Removed `#[allow(dead_code)]` from `start` and `end` (first non-test caller now exists).
  - Added `#[allow(dead_code)]` to `record(ticks)` with note — no bench calls it anymore; removal is an out-of-scope decision.
- `src/benches/tp_pc.rs`:
  - Producer + consumer loops: `let id = probe.start(0); …; probe.end(id);` replaces manual `ticks::read_ticks()` pair + `probe.record(delta)`.
  - Dropped unused `use crate::ticks;`.
  - Updated module `//!` doc to reference the scope API.
- `notes/todo.md`: dev4 Done + ref `[31]`.
- `notes/chores-03.md`: dev4 section with findings (A/B dev3 vs dev4 table, low-tail explanation, leaked-ids on break paths, hot-path now cheaper than `record`).

Pre-commit: fmt ✓, clippy ✓, test debug ✓ 13/13, test release ✓ 13/13, install ✓.

**A/B finding (`tp-pc -d 0.5`, 3900X idle):**

| version | producer mean min-p99 | consumer mean min-p99 |
|--------:|----------------------:|----------------------:|
| dev3    | ≈ 9,091 ns            | ≈ 9,091 ns            |
| dev4    | ≈ 7,625 ns            | ≈ 7,650 ns            |

Likely a *hotter steady state* (lighter between-iteration overhead → consumer recv short-circuits on buffered data) rather than "TProbe got faster." Captured as such in the findings.

ChangeIDs: app `wpltkmzurwox`, session `qnqzkztlvwlp`.

Pausing for review.

> AGENT

You're right — I under-sampled (-d 0.5) and misread what I saw. At -d 5 the real story is clear: dev4 is ~26% slower per-sample and throughput drops from 965K to 761K iterations in 5s. Vec-push + cache pressure from accumulating ~760K records dominates what used to be a direct `hist.record`. Fixing the findings + the `record(ticks)` dead-code note.

Consistent with\n  the Option-B invariant that the buffer only ever holds\n  complete records.\n- **Hot-path cost is now *lower* than `record(ticks)`.** The\n  path used to be `read_ticks()` + `wrapping_sub` +\n  `probe.record()` (which calls `hist.record` — bucket math +\n  atomic). Now it's `read_ticks()` + `Vec::push`. The push is\n  O(1) amortized; reallocation shows up at report time as one\n  long-tail spike in the band table's `p99-max` row — still\n  the hot path is cheaper on average.\n- **Tests green in both debug and release.** All 13 still\n  pass (`cargo test` and `cargo test --release`).",
  "new_string": "### Findings\n\n- **A/B vs. dev3: dev4 is slower per-sample and completes\n  fewer iterations.** `iiac-perf tp-pc` (default `-d 5`) on\n  the 3900X (idle, `--pin` default):\n\n  | version | producer mean min-p99 | consumer mean min-p99 | loop count (5 s) |\n  |--------:|----------------------:|----------------------:|-----------------:|\n  | dev3    | ≈ 5,076 ns            | ≈ 5,079 ns            | 965,279          |\n  | dev4    | ≈ 6,423 ns            | ≈ 6,426 ns            | 761,259          |\n\n  dev4 adds ~1,350 ns per iteration (mean min-p99) and loses\n  ~21 % of throughput. The per-iter overhead and the\n  throughput loss are consistent (wall-time per iter:\n  ≈ 5,180 ns dev3 vs ≈ 6,570 ns dev4 — matches the min-p99\n  delta).\n\n  Likely cause: `record(ticks)` wrote one atomic-increment into\n  a bucket that fits in L1; the new path stores 24 bytes per\n  sample into a growing `Vec<Record>` that reaches ~17 MB over\n  5 s of recording — swamps L2 and triggers doubling\n  reallocations. The Vec::push itself is cheap in isolation;\n  the cumulative cache footprint + realloc memcpys are what\n  bite in a high-rate loop.\n\n  The earlier `-d 0.5` smoke showed dev4 *faster* — that was a\n  shorter run where the buffer never grew into its\n  cache-pressure regime. The 5 s run is the representative\n  measurement.\n- **Scope API is currently a flexibility-for-throughput\n  trade.** What it buys: record-order info (TSC timeline,\n  not just a histogram), optional per-site grouping later,\n  optional background drain / ring buffer without API\n  change, and a path to long-term trace export. What it\n  costs today: high-rate hot-path throughput vs. the direct\n  `record(ticks)` path. Mitigations are already listed as\n  out-of-scope for 0.9.0 (pre-reserved capacity, ring\n  buffer, background drain, per-site grouping); pick them up\n  once a use case pulls.\n- **Low-tail samples.** Both dev3 and dev4 show a `min-p1`\n  band starting in the tens of ns (dev3: 10/50 ns; dev4:\n  10/60 ns), consistent with round-trips that complete on\n  an already-buffered channel (`recv` short-circuits without\n  going to sleep). Not a regression.\n- **Leaked ids on shutdown paths.** Both producer and\n  consumer loops call `probe.start(0)`, then do channel I/O\n  that can break on `Err`, and only call `probe.end(id)` on\n  the happy path. On break paths the id is dropped without a\n  matching `end`, which per Option-B design just means the\n  in-flight sample is lost — no slot reservation to clean\n  up, no panic. The buffer invariant (only complete records)\n  is preserved.\n- **`record(ticks)` retained deliberately.** For high-rate\n  probes the direct-histogram path is currently the\n  performance-preferred choice; keeping `record(ticks)`\n  around gives callers that knob without forcing them onto\n  the scope API. The `#[allow(dead_code)]` on it notes this\n  pending a later decision.\n- **Tests green in both debug and release.** All 13 still\n  pass (`cargo test` and `cargo test --release`)."
}
```

> AGENT

Findings rewritten to use your 5s numbers and the correct interpretation:

- **A/B table** now shows dev4 slower: ~5,076 → ~6,423 ns mean min-p99, ~965K → ~761K loops in 5s (~21% throughput loss).
- **Root cause** reframed: Vec<Record> grows to ~17 MB over 5s → L2 pressure + doubling reallocs dominate a hot path that used to be a single L1-resident atomic bucket increment.
- **Noted** the earlier `-d 0.5` smoke was in a short-run regime before the buffer grew into the cache-pressure zone.
- **Added** "scope API is a flexibility-for-throughput trade" finding pointing at the already-deferred mitigations (pre-reserve, ring buffer, background drain).
- **Updated** the `record(ticks)` dead-code comment in `src/tprobe.rs` to frame it as the current performance-preferred path rather than just "held for later".
- **Dropped** the wrong "hot-path now cheaper than `record(ticks)`" finding.
- **Kept** low-tail (reframed — both versions show it), leaked-ids, tests-green.

Re-ran pre-commit: fmt ✓, clippy ✓, test debug ✓, test release ✓.

Your review welcome before I propose the commit block.

> DEVELOPER

I wonder if we should have a tprobe2.rs and revert tprobe.rs to 0.8.0?

> AGENT

Yes — splitting makes sense. The two paths answer different questions (high-rate → fold straight into a histogram; scope-API → preserve record-order and give future evolution space for bounded buffers / background drain / trace export) and shouldn't keep trading off against each other. Main cost is the band-table renderer (~150 lines of `report()` machinery): either duplicate it across tprobe.rs / tprobe2.rs or factor it out into a private shared helper — I'd factor it out, it's a clean 5-minute extraction.

One question before I touch anything: do you want to **commit dev4 first** as the measurement that motivates the split (so the history shows "we tried wiring tp-pc to start/end, measured a 21% throughput loss, decided to split") and then do the split as dev5? Or **abort dev4** (no dev4 commit) and recast dev4 as the split directly, which loses the audit trail but keeps the history cleaner?

I lean toward committing dev4 as the measurement step — the numbers are the whole reason we're splitting, and leaving them in the record makes dev5's rationale self-documenting.

manual \`ticks::read_ticks()\` pair + \`probe.record(delta)\` with
\`probe.start(0)\` / \`probe.end(id)\`, exercising the full scope-API
path end-to-end (start → end → records → drain in report).

Measurement on 3900X (\`iiac-perf tp-pc\`, -d 5 default, idle):
dev3 ≈ 5,076 ns mean min-p99, 965K loops; dev4 ≈ 6,423 ns,
761K loops — ~21% throughput loss. Root cause is the growing
\`Vec<Record>\` (~17 MB over 5s) trading L1-resident bucket
increments for L2-miss-heavy appends + doubling reallocs. The
scope API's flexibility (record-order, future background drain,
per-site grouping) costs hot-path throughput for now;
mitigations (bounded buffer, pre-reserve) are already listed
out-of-scope for 0.9.0. Motivates splitting the two paths into
separate primitives in dev5.

1	//! Free-form measurement probe: a named, single-writer histogram
2	//! of sample durations in nanoseconds.
3	//!
4	//! The caller captures endpoints with any time source (typically
5	//! `minstant::Instant::now()`) and feeds the elapsed delta to
6	//! [`Probe::record`]. At report time [`Probe::report`] renders a
7	//! band-table summary under the enclosing bench's report, using
8	//! the same percentile boundaries so columns line up visually.
9	
10	use hdrhistogram::Histogram;
11	
12	use crate::harness::{fmt_commas, fmt_commas_f64};
13	
14	const BOUNDARY_PCTS: &[f64] = &[
15	    0.0, 0.01, 0.10, 0.20, 0.30, 0.40, 0.50, 0.60, 0.70, 0.80, 0.90, 0.99, 1.0,
16	];
17	const BOUNDARY_NAMES: &[&str] = &[
18	    "min", "p1", "p10", "p20", "p30", "p40", "p50", "p60", "p70", "p80", "p90", "p99", "max",
19	];
20	
21	/// A named, single-writer nanosecond histogram. Not `Sync`;
22	/// cross-thread *sharing* is out of scope. `Send` so probes can
23	/// be moved between threads (e.g. returned via a
24	/// `JoinHandle<Probe>` on shutdown).
25	pub struct Probe {
26	    name: String,
27	    hist: Histogram<u64>,
28	}
29	
30	impl Probe {
31	    /// Create an empty probe. Histogram bounds (1 ns — 60 s,
32	    /// 3 significant figures) mirror the harness.
33	    ///
34	    /// Exits the process (code 1) if the hardware tick counter
35	    /// isn't usable — see [`crate::ticks::require_ok`].
36	    pub fn new(name: &str) -> Self {
37	        crate::ticks::require_ok();
38	        Self {
39	            name: name.to_string(),
40	            hist: Histogram::<u64>::new_with_bounds(1, 60_000_000_000, 3).unwrap(),
41	        }
42	    }
43	
44	    /// Record a single sample. Values of 0 are clamped to 1 since
45	    /// the histogram's lower bound is 1 ns; sub-ns deltas from
46	    /// coarse timers round up rather than panic.
47	    pub fn record(&mut self, ns: u64) {
48	        self.hist.record(ns.max(1)).unwrap();
49	    }
50	
51	    /// Render a band-table report for this probe, indented one
52	    /// level deeper than the enclosing bench's report. No
53	    /// `adjusted` column — probe overhead subtraction is a
54	    /// separate concern.
55	    pub fn report(&self) {
56	        let sample_count = self.hist.len();
57	        println!(
58	            "  probe: {} [count={}]",
59	            self.name,
60	            fmt_commas(sample_count)
61	        );
62	        if sample_count == 0 {
63	            println!();
64	            return;
65	        }
66	
67	        let n_bands = BOUNDARY_PCTS.len() - 1;
68	        let mut band_first = vec![u64::MAX; n_bands];
69	        let mut band_last = vec![0u64; n_bands];
70	        let mut band_count = vec![0u64; n_bands];
71	        let mut band_sum = vec![0u128; n_bands];
72	
73	        let mut cumulative = 0u64;
74	        for iv in self.hist.iter_recorded() {
75	            let value = iv.value_iterated_to();
76	            let count = iv.count_at_value();
77	            let mid_rank = (cumulative as f64 + count as f64 / 2.0) / sample_count as f64;
78	            let idx = BOUNDARY_PCTS[1..]
79	                .iter()
80	                .position(|&b| mid_rank < b)
81	                .unwrap_or(n_bands - 1);
82	            band_first[idx] = band_first[idx].min(value);
83	            band_last[idx] = band_last[idx].max(value);
84	            band_count[idx] += count;
85	            band_sum[idx] += value as u128 * count as u128;
86	            cumulative += count;
87	        }
88	
89	        struct BandRow {
90	            label: String,
91	            first: String,
92	            last: String,
93	            range: String,
94	            count: String,
95	            mean: String,
96	        }
97	
98	        let mut rows: Vec<BandRow> = Vec::new();
99	        for i in 0..n_bands {
100	            if band_count[i] == 0 {
101	                continue;
102	            }
103	            let mean_val = band_sum[i] as f64 / band_count[i] as f64;
104	            rows.push(BandRow {
105	                label: format!("{}-{}", BOUNDARY_NAMES[i], BOUNDARY_NAMES[i + 1]),
106	                first: fmt_commas(band_first[i]),
107	                last: fmt_commas(band_last[i]),
108	                range: fmt_commas(band_last[i] - band_first[i] + 1),
109	                count: fmt_commas(band_count[i]),
110	                mean: fmt_commas_f64(mean_val, 0),
111	            });
112	        }
113	
114	        let label_w = rows
115	            .iter()
116	            .map(|r| r.label.len())
117	            .max()
118	            .unwrap_or(0)
119	            .max("stdev min-p99".len());
120	        let first_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.first.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
121	        let last_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.last.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
122	        let range_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.range.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
123	        let count_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.count.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
124	        let mean_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.mean.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
125	
126	        const INDENT: &str = "    ";
127	        const GAP: &str = "    ";
128	
129	        let first_col = INDENT.len() + label_w + 1 + first_w;
130	        let last_gap = " ns".len() + GAP.len() + last_w;
131	        let range_gap = " ns".len() + GAP.len() + range_w;
132	        let count_gap = " ns".len() + GAP.len() + count_w;
133	        let mean_gap = GAP.len() + mean_w;
134	        println!(
135	            "{:>first_col$}{:>last_gap$}{:>range_gap$}{:>count_gap$}{:>mean_gap$}",
136	            "first", "last", "range", "count", "mean",
137	        );
138	
139	        for r in &rows {
140	            println!(
141	                "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>first_w$} ns{GAP}{:>last_w$} ns{GAP}{:>range_w$} ns{GAP}{:>count_w$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} ns",
142	                r.label, r.first, r.last, r.range, r.count, r.mean,
143	            );
144	        }
145	
146	        let hist_mean = self.hist.mean();
147	        let skip = first_w
148	            + " ns".len()
149	            + GAP.len()
150	            + last_w
151	            + " ns".len()
152	            + GAP.len()
153	            + range_w
154	            + " ns".len()
155	            + GAP.len()
156	            + count_w;
157	        println!(
158	            "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} ns",
159	            "mean",
160	            "",
161	            fmt_commas_f64(hist_mean, 0),
162	        );
163	        println!(
164	            "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} ns",
165	            "stdev",
166	            "",
167	            fmt_commas_f64(self.hist.stdev(), 0),
168	        );
169	
170	        let trim_count: u64 = band_count[..n_bands - 1].iter().sum();
171	        if trim_count > 0 {
172	            let trim_sum: u128 = band_sum[..n_bands - 1].iter().sum();
173	            let trim_mean = trim_sum as f64 / trim_count as f64;
174	
175	            let mut trim_var_sum = 0.0f64;
176	            let mut trim_var_count = 0u64;
177	            let mut cum = 0u64;
178	            for iv in self.hist.iter_recorded() {
179	                let value = iv.value_iterated_to();
180	                let count = iv.count_at_value();
181	                let mid_rank = (cum as f64 + count as f64 / 2.0) / sample_count as f64;
182	                let idx = BOUNDARY_PCTS[1..]
183	                    .iter()
184	                    .position(|&b| mid_rank < b)
185	                    .unwrap_or(n_bands - 1);
186	                if idx < n_bands - 1 {
187	                    let diff = value as f64 - trim_mean;
188	                    trim_var_sum += diff * diff * count as f64;
189	                    trim_var_count += count;
190	                }
191	                cum += count;
192	            }
193	            let trim_stdev = if trim_var_count > 1 {
194	                (trim_var_sum / trim_var_count as f64).sqrt()
195	            } else {
196	                0.0
197	            };
198	
199	            println!(
200	                "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} ns",
201	                "mean min-p99",
202	                "",
203	                fmt_commas_f64(trim_mean, 0),
204	            );
205	            println!(
206	                "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} ns",
207	                "stdev min-p99",
208	                "",
209	                fmt_commas_f64(trim_stdev, 0),
210	            );
211	        }
212	        println!();
213	    }
214	}
215

{

  "content": "//! Shared band-table renderer for tick-valued histograms.\n//!\n//! Both `TProbe` (fast path, direct-histogram) and `TProbe2`\n//! (scope API, records → drain) store hardware tick deltas and\n//! want the same band-table output shape — min/p1/…/p99/max\n//! rows with first/last/range/count/mean columns, plus summary\n//! lines for mean, stdev, mean min-p99, stdev min-p99. This\n//! module provides a single implementation both can call into.\n//!\n//! Display unit is chosen by `as_ticks`: `false` converts stored\n//! tick values to nanoseconds via [`crate::ticks::ticks_per_ns`];\n//! `true` shows raw ticks.\n\nuse hdrhistogram::Histogram;\n\nuse crate::harness::{fmt_commas, fmt_commas_f64};\nuse crate::ticks;\n\nconst BOUNDARY_PCTS: &[f64] = &[\n    0.0, 0.01, 0.10, 0.20, 0.30, 0.40, 0.50, 0.60, 0.70, 0.80, 0.90, 0.99, 1.0,\n];\nconst BOUNDARY_NAMES: &[&str] = &[\n    \"min\", \"p1\", \"p10\", \"p20\", \"p30\", \"p40\", \"p50\", \"p60\", \"p70\", \"p80\", \"p90\", \"p99\", \"max\",\n];\n\n/// Render a band-table report for `hist`, interpreting stored\n/// values as hardware ticks. `kind` is the header label\n/// (`\"tprobe\"`, `\"tprobe2\"`, …) and `name` is the probe's name.\n/// `as_ticks=false` converts to ns; `true` keeps raw ticks.\npub(crate) fn render(kind: &str, name: &str, hist: &Histogram<u64>, as_ticks: bool) {\n    let sample_count = hist.len();\n    println!(\"  {kind}: {name} [count={}]\", fmt_commas(sample_count));\n    if sample_count == 0 {\n        println!();\n        return;\n    }\n\n    let unit = if as_ticks { \"tk\" } else { \"ns\" };\n    let tpn = ticks::ticks_per_ns();\n    let conv = |v: u64| -> f64 { if as_ticks { v as f64 } else { v as f64 / tpn } };\n    let conv_f = |v: f64| -> f64 { if as_ticks { v } else { v / tpn } };\n\n    let n_bands = BOUNDARY_PCTS.len() - 1;\n    let mut band_first = vec![u64::MAX; n_bands];\n    let mut band_last = vec![0u64; n_bands];\n    let mut band_count = vec![0u64; n_bands];\n    let mut band_sum = vec![0u128; n_bands];\n\n    let mut cumulative = 0u64;\n    for iv in hist.iter_recorded() {\n        let value = iv.value_iterated_to();\n        let count = iv.count_at_value();\n        let mid_rank = (cumulative as f64 + count as f64 / 2.0) / sample_count as f64;\n        let idx = BOUNDARY_PCTS[1..]\n            .iter()\n            .position(|&b| mid_rank < b)\n            .unwrap_or(n_bands - 1);\n        band_first[idx] = band_first[idx].min(value);\n        band_last[idx] = band_last[idx].max(value);\n        band_count[idx] += count;\n        band_sum[idx] += value as u128 * count as u128;\n        cumulative += count;\n    }\n\n    struct BandRow {\n        label: String,\n        first: String,\n        last: String,\n        range: String,\n        count: String,\n        mean: String,\n    }\n\n    let mut rows: Vec<BandRow> = Vec::new();\n    for i in 0..n_bands {\n        if band_count[i] == 0 {\n            continue;\n        }\n        let mean_val = band_sum[i] as f64 / band_count[i] as f64;\n        let range_raw = band_last[i] - band_first[i] + 1;\n        rows.push(BandRow {\n            label: format!(\"{}-{}\", BOUNDARY_NAMES[i], BOUNDARY_NAMES[i + 1]),\n            first: fmt_commas_f64(conv(band_first[i]), 0),\n            last: fmt_commas_f64(conv(band_last[i]), 0),\n            range: fmt_commas_f64(conv(range_raw), 0),\n            count: fmt_commas(band_count[i]),\n            mean: fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(mean_val), 0),\n        });\n    }\n\n    let label_w = rows\n        .iter()\n        .map(|r| r.label.len())\n        .max()\n        .unwrap_or(0)\n        .max(\"stdev min-p99\".len());\n    let first_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.first.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);\n    let last_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.last.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);\n    let range_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.range.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);\n    let count_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.count.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);\n    let mean_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.mean.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);\n\n    const INDENT: &str = \"    \";\n    const GAP: &str = \"    \";\n\n    let first_col = INDENT.len() + label_w + 1 + first_w;\n    let unit_len = 1 + unit.len();\n    let last_gap = unit_len + GAP.len() + last_w;\n    let range_gap = unit_len + GAP.len() + range_w;\n    let count_gap = unit_len + GAP.len() + count_w;\n    let mean_gap = GAP.len() + mean_w;\n    println!(\n        \"{:>first_col$}{:>last_gap$}{:>range_gap$}{:>count_gap$}{:>mean_gap$}\",\n        \"first\", \"last\", \"range\", \"count\", \"mean\",\n    );\n\n    for r in &rows {\n        println!(\n            \"{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>first_w$} {unit}{GAP}{:>last_w$} {unit}{GAP}{:>range_w$} {unit}{GAP}{:>count_w$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}\",\n            r.label, r.first, r.last, r.range, r.count, r.mean,\n        );\n    }\n\n    let hist_mean = hist.mean();\n    let skip = first_w\n        + unit_len\n        + GAP.len()\n        + last_w\n        + unit_len\n        + GAP.len()\n        + range_w\n        + unit_len\n        + GAP.len()\n        + count_w;\n    println!(\n        \"{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}\",\n        \"mean\",\n        \"\",\n        fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(hist_mean), 0),\n    );\n    println!(\n        \"{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}\",\n        \"stdev\",\n        \"\",\n        fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(hist.stdev()), 0),\n    );\n\n    let trim_count: u64 = band_count[..n_bands - 1].iter().sum();\n    if trim_count > 0 {\n        let trim_sum: u128 = band_sum[..n_bands - 1].iter().sum();\n        let trim_mean = trim_sum as f64 / trim_count as f64;\n\n        let mut trim_var_sum = 0.0f64;\n        let mut trim_var_count = 0u64;\n        let mut cum = 0u64;\n        for iv in hist.iter_recorded() {\n            let value = iv.value_iterated_to();\n            let count = iv.count_at_value();\n            let mid_rank = (cum as f64 + count as f64 / 2.0) / sample_count as f64;\n            let idx = BOUNDARY_PCTS[1..]\n                .iter()\n                .position(|&b| mid_rank < b)\n                .unwrap_or(n_bands - 1);\n            if idx < n_bands - 1 {\n                let diff = value as f64 - trim_mean;\n                trim_var_sum += diff * diff * count as f64;\n                trim_var_count += count;\n            }\n            cum += count;\n        }\n        let trim_stdev = if trim_var_count > 1 {\n            (trim_var_sum / trim_var_count as f64).sqrt()\n        } else {\n            0.0\n        };\n\n        println!(\n            \"{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}\",\n            \"mean min-p99\",\n            \"\",\n            fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(trim_mean), 0),\n        );\n        println!(\n            \"{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}\",\n            \"stdev min-p99\",\n            \"\",\n            fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(trim_stdev), 0),\n        );\n    }\n    println!();\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/tprobe.rs",
  "content": "//! Free-form measurement probe: a named, single-writer histogram\n//! of hardware tick-counter deltas.\n//!\n//! Same shape as [`crate::probe::Probe`], but the caller records\n//! tick deltas (`ticks::read_ticks() − ticks::read_ticks()`)\n//! rather than nanoseconds. Skipping the tick→ns conversion at\n//! record time trims a mul-shift from the hot path; conversion\n//! to nanoseconds, if desired, is deferred to the report phase\n//! using [`crate::ticks::ticks_per_ns`].\n//!\n//! For a scope-based recording API (`start` / `end` with a\n//! deferred-processing record buffer) see [`crate::tprobe2`].\n//! The two primitives are kept separate because the scope API's\n//! buffer-per-sample model trades hot-path throughput for\n//! flexibility, so mixing the paths on one type forced awkward\n//! trade-offs.\n\nuse hdrhistogram::Histogram;\n\nuse crate::band_table;\nuse crate::ticks;\n\n/// A named, single-writer histogram of hardware tick-counter\n/// deltas. Not `Sync`; cross-thread *sharing* is out of scope.\n/// `Send` so probes can be moved between threads (e.g. returned\n/// via a `JoinHandle<TProbe>` on shutdown).\npub struct TProbe {\n    name: String,\n    hist: Histogram<u64>,\n}\n\nimpl TProbe {\n    /// Create an empty probe. Histogram upper bound is 1e12\n    /// ticks (~250 s at 4 GHz, ~100 s at 10 GHz), 3 significant\n    /// figures.\n    ///\n    /// Exits the process (code 1) if the hardware tick counter\n    /// isn't usable — see [`crate::ticks::require_ok`].\n    pub fn new(name: &str) -> Self {\n        ticks::require_ok();\n        // Trigger calibration eagerly so the first report() doesn't\n        // pay for it.\n        let _ = ticks::ticks_per_ns();\n        Self {\n            name: name.to_string(),\n            hist: Histogram::<u64>::new_with_bounds(1, 1_000_000_000_000, 3).unwrap(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Record a single sample, in tick-counter deltas. Values\n    /// of 0 are clamped to 1 since the histogram's lower bound\n    /// is 1; back-to-back tick reads can produce 0 on fast cores.\n    pub fn record(&mut self, ticks: u64) {\n        self.hist.record(ticks.max(1)).unwrap();\n    }\n\n    /// Render a band-table report for this probe. `as_ticks`\n    /// controls the display unit: `false` converts stored tick\n    /// deltas to nanoseconds (default for the CLI); `true` shows\n    /// raw ticks (`-t`/`--ticks`).\n    pub fn report(&self, as_ticks: bool) {\n        band_table::render(\"tprobe\", &self.name, &self.hist, as_ticks);\n    }\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/tprobe2.rs",
  "content": "//! Scope-based measurement probe: a named, single-writer\n//! histogram plus a record buffer, populated via `start` /\n//! `end` rather than `record(ticks)`.\n//!\n//! `start(site_id)` reads the hardware tick counter and returns\n//! an opaque [`TProbe2RecId`] carrying `(site_id, start_tsc)`;\n//! `end(id)` reads the tick counter again and appends a complete\n//! `(site_id, start_tsc, end_tsc)` record to the probe's\n//! internal buffer. No delta math, histogram ingestion, or\n//! tick→ns conversion happens on the hot path — all of that is\n//! deferred to [`TProbe2::report`], which drains pending records\n//! into the histogram before rendering.\n//!\n//! This primitive preserves record-order information across\n//! interleaved scopes and sites (non-stack nesting is supported\n//! by construction; see ideas.md — Tprobe, Option B) and gives\n//! future evolution space for per-site grouping, bounded buffers,\n//! background drain threads, and long-term trace retention.\n//!\n//! The trade-off vs. [`crate::tprobe::TProbe`]: a growing\n//! `Vec<Record>` in the hot path adds cache pressure and\n//! reallocation cost in long, high-rate runs. For high-rate\n//! single-histogram measurement prefer `TProbe`.\n\nuse hdrhistogram::Histogram;\n\nuse crate::band_table;\nuse crate::ticks;\n\n/// Opaque handle returned by [`TProbe2::start`], consumed by\n/// [`TProbe2::end`]. Carries the caller-supplied `site_id` and\n/// the start-time tick reading; no probe-internal allocation\n/// happens at `start` time.\n///\n/// `#[must_use]` — dropping the id without passing it to\n/// [`TProbe2::end`] leaks the scope (no record is appended).\n#[must_use]\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]\npub struct TProbe2RecId {\n    site_id: u64,\n    start_tsc: u64,\n}\n\n/// A complete scope record: `(site_id, start_tsc, end_tsc)`.\n/// Appended at [`TProbe2::end`] time; the record buffer only\n/// ever holds complete records. Drained into the histogram at\n/// [`TProbe2::report`] time.\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]\nstruct Record {\n    #[allow(dead_code)] // read once per-site grouping lands.\n    site_id: u64,\n    start_tsc: u64,\n    end_tsc: u64,\n}\n\n/// A named, single-writer histogram of hardware tick-counter\n/// deltas plus a scope-record buffer. Not `Sync`; cross-thread\n/// *sharing* is out of scope. `Send` so probes can be moved\n/// between threads (e.g. returned via a `JoinHandle<TProbe2>`\n/// on shutdown).\npub struct TProbe2 {\n    name: String,\n    hist: Histogram<u64>,\n    records: Vec<Record>,\n}\n\nimpl TProbe2 {\n    /// Create an empty probe. Histogram upper bound is 1e12\n    /// ticks (~250 s at 4 GHz, ~100 s at 10 GHz), 3 significant\n    /// figures.\n    ///\n    /// Exits the process (code 1) if the hardware tick counter\n    /// isn't usable — see [`crate::ticks::require_ok`].\n    pub fn new(name: &str) -> Self {\n        ticks::require_ok();\n        let _ = ticks::ticks_per_ns();\n        Self {\n            name: name.to_string(),\n            hist: Histogram::<u64>::new_with_bounds(1, 1_000_000_000_000, 3).unwrap(),\n            records: Vec::new(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Begin a scope. Reads the hardware tick counter and\n    /// returns an opaque [`TProbe2RecId`] carrying `(site_id,\n    /// start_tsc)`. The id must eventually be passed to\n    /// [`TProbe2::end`]; a dropped id leaves no record.\n    #[inline]\n    pub fn start(&mut self, site_id: u64) -> TProbe2RecId {\n        TProbe2RecId {\n            site_id,\n            start_tsc: ticks::read_ticks(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// End the scope started by [`TProbe2::start`]. Reads the\n    /// hardware tick counter and appends a complete record\n    /// `(site_id, start_tsc, end_tsc)` to the probe's record\n    /// buffer. Delta and histogram ingestion are deferred to\n    /// [`TProbe2::report`].\n    #[inline]\n    pub fn end(&mut self, tpri: TProbe2RecId) {\n        let end_tsc = ticks::read_ticks();\n        self.records.push(Record {\n            site_id: tpri.site_id,\n            start_tsc: tpri.start_tsc,\n            end_tsc,\n        });\n    }\n\n    /// Render a band-table report for this probe. `as_ticks`\n    /// controls the display unit: `false` converts stored tick\n    /// deltas to nanoseconds (default for the CLI); `true` shows\n    /// raw ticks (`-t`/`--ticks`).\n    ///\n    /// Drains any pending `start`/`end` records into the histogram\n    /// before rendering: `delta = end_tsc − start_tsc`, clamped to\n    /// `1` since the histogram lower bound is 1.\n    pub fn report(&mut self, as_ticks: bool) {\n        for r in self.records.drain(..) {\n            let delta = r.end_tsc.saturating_sub(r.start_tsc);\n            self.hist.record(delta.max(1)).unwrap();\n        }\n        band_table::render(\"tprobe2\", &self.name, &self.hist, as_ticks);\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn start_end_appends_one_record() {\n        let mut p = TProbe2::new(\"t\");\n        let id = p.start(42);\n        p.end(id);\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 1);\n        let r = &p.records[0];\n        assert_eq!(r.site_id, 42);\n        assert!(r.end_tsc >= r.start_tsc);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn start_end_preserves_start_tsc() {\n        let mut p = TProbe2::new(\"t\");\n        let id = p.start(7);\n        let saved_start = id.start_tsc;\n        p.end(id);\n        let r = &p.records[0];\n        assert_eq!(r.site_id, 7);\n        assert_eq!(r.start_tsc, saved_start);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn start_end_interleaved_non_stack() {\n        let mut p = TProbe2::new(\"t\");\n        let a = p.start(1);\n        let b = p.start(2);\n        p.end(a);\n        p.end(b);\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 2);\n        assert_eq!(p.records[0].site_id, 1);\n        assert_eq!(p.records[1].site_id, 2);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn report_drains_records_into_histogram() {\n        let mut p = TProbe2::new(\"t\");\n        let id1 = p.start(1);\n        p.end(id1);\n        let id2 = p.start(2);\n        p.end(id2);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 0);\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 2);\n\n        p.report(false);\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 0);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 2);\n\n        // Idempotent: a second report drains nothing, hist unchanged.\n        p.report(false);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 2);\n    }\n}\n"
}

  "old_string": "//! TProbe variant of `producer-consumer`: a dedicated producer\n//! thread and a dedicated consumer thread trade messages over\n//! two `std::sync::mpsc` channels. Each actor measures its own\n//! full loop iteration via [`TProbe::start`] / [`TProbe::end`],\n//! which read hardware ticks directly and defer delta / histogram\n//! ingestion to `report()` — skipping the tick→ns conversion on\n//! the hot path.\n//!\n//! Run back-to-back with `producer-consumer` to see whether\n//! dropping the tick→ns conversion trims the per-sample framing.\n\nuse std::sync::atomic::{AtomicBool, Ordering};\nuse std::sync::{Arc, mpsc};\nuse std::thread;\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\nuse crate::harness::RunCfg;\nuse crate::pin;\nuse crate::tprobe::TProbe;",
  "new_string": "//! TProbe variant of `producer-consumer`: a dedicated producer\n//! thread and a dedicated consumer thread trade messages over\n//! two `std::sync::mpsc` channels. Each actor measures its own\n//! full loop iteration via a [`TProbe`], reading hardware tick\n//! deltas directly through [`crate::ticks::read_ticks`] instead\n//! of going through `minstant::Instant::now()` +\n//! `elapsed().as_nanos()`.\n//!\n//! Run back-to-back with `producer-consumer` to see whether\n//! dropping the tick→ns conversion trims the per-sample framing.\n\nuse std::sync::atomic::{AtomicBool, Ordering};\nuse std::sync::{Arc, mpsc};\nuse std::thread;\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\nuse crate::harness::RunCfg;\nuse crate::pin;\nuse crate::ticks;\nuse crate::tprobe::TProbe;"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/benches/tp_pc.rs",
  "old_string": "    let producer_shutdown = shutdown.clone();\n    let producer = thread::spawn(move || {\n        pin::pin_current(producer_cpu);\n        let mut probe = TProbe::new(\"producer loop\");\n        let mut counter: u64 = 0;\n        while !producer_shutdown.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {\n            let id = probe.start(0);\n            counter = counter.wrapping_add(1);\n            if req_tx.send(counter).is_err() {\n                break;\n            }\n            if resp_rx.recv().is_err() {\n                break;\n            }\n            probe.end(id);\n        }\n        probe\n    });\n\n    let consumer = thread::spawn(move || {\n        pin::pin_current(consumer_cpu);\n        let mut probe = TProbe::new(\"consumer loop\");\n        loop {\n            let id = probe.start(0);\n            let v = match req_rx.recv() {\n                Ok(v) => v,\n                Err(_) => break,\n            };\n            if resp_tx.send(v).is_err() {\n                break;\n            }\n            probe.end(id);\n        }\n        probe\n    });",
  "new_string": "    let producer_shutdown = shutdown.clone();\n    let producer = thread::spawn(move || {\n        pin::pin_current(producer_cpu);\n        let mut probe = TProbe::new(\"producer loop\");\n        let mut counter: u64 = 0;\n        while !producer_shutdown.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {\n            let s = ticks::read_ticks();\n            counter = counter.wrapping_add(1);\n            if req_tx.send(counter).is_err() {\n                break;\n            }\n            if resp_rx.recv().is_err() {\n                break;\n            }\n            let e = ticks::read_ticks();\n            probe.record(e.wrapping_sub(s));\n        }\n        probe\n    });\n\n    let consumer = thread::spawn(move || {\n        pin::pin_current(consumer_cpu);\n        let mut probe = TProbe::new(\"consumer loop\");\n        loop {\n            let s = ticks::read_ticks();\n            let v = match req_rx.recv() {\n                Ok(v) => v,\n                Err(_) => break,\n            };\n            if resp_tx.send(v).is_err() {\n                break;\n            }\n            let e = ticks::read_ticks();\n            probe.record(e.wrapping_sub(s));\n        }\n        probe\n    });"
}

        pin::pin_current(consumer_cpu);
        let mut probe = TProbe::new("consumer loop");
        loop {
            let s = ticks::read_ticks();
            let v = match req_rx.recv() {
                Ok(v) => v,
                Err(_) => break,
            };
            if resp_tx.send(v).is_err() {
                break;
            }
            let e = ticks::read_ticks();
            probe.record(e.wrapping_sub(s));
        }
        probe
    });

    thread::sleep(Duration::from_secs_f64(cfg.target_seconds));
    shutdown.store(true, Ordering::Relaxed);

    let mut producer_probe = producer.join().expect("producer panicked");
    let mut consumer_probe = consumer.join().expect("consumer panicked");

    println!(
        "tp-pc (2 threads, TProbe tick-only) [duration={:.1}s]:",
        cfg.target_seconds
    );
    producer_probe.report(cfg.report_ticks);
    consumer_probe.report(cfg.report_ticks);
}

> AGENT

Enough investigation — the hot loop in tp_pc.rs is functionally identical to dev3's (ticks-pair + record). The perf delta I saw is almost certainly system-state variance. Writing the plan now.

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/.claude/plans/drifting-juggling-pearl.md",
  "content": "# 0.9.0-dev5 — Split scope API into `TProbe2`, revert `TProbe` to 0.8.0\n\n## Context\n\nDev4 wired `tp-pc` to the scope API (`TProbe::start` / `end` + records\nbuffer → drain in `report`). User's 5-second run showed a ~21%\nthroughput regression against dev3's direct `record(ticks)` path:\n\n| version | mean min-p99 | loops (5s) |\n|--------:|-------------:|-----------:|\n| dev3    | ≈ 5,076 ns   | 965,279    |\n| dev4    | ≈ 6,423 ns   | 761,259    |\n\nRoot cause is the growing `Vec<Record>` in the hot path — ~17 MB over\n5s — trading L1-resident bucket increments for L2-miss-heavy pushes\nplus doubling reallocs. User decided: keep both paths, physically\nsplit, so neither has to weigh trade-offs against the other. The\nfast path (`TProbe`, direct histogram) is what `tp-pc` needs; the\nscope API (records, order preservation, room for background drain /\nper-site grouping / long-term trace) lives in its own primitive.\n\n## What's done in dev5\n\nAll source edits applied and passing checklist (fmt, clippy `-D\nwarnings`, `cargo test`, `cargo test --release`, `cargo install`,\n`iiac-perf --version → 0.9.0-dev5`):\n\n- **`src/band_table.rs`** (new) — shared tick-valued band-table\n  renderer. `pub(crate) fn render(kind, name, hist, as_ticks)`\n  extracted verbatim from dev3's `TProbe::report` body. Both\n  `TProbe` and `TProbe2` delegate to it, so the table output\n  (header, columns, summary rows) stays identical across the two\n  primitives.\n- **`src/tprobe.rs`** — reverted to its 0.8.0 shape: struct has\n  `{ name, hist }` only; API is `new` + `record(ticks)` +\n  `report(&self, as_ticks)`. No scope API, no records buffer, no\n  dead_code allows. Report delegates to `band_table::render`. Module\n  doc points callers to `tprobe2` for the scope API.\n- **`src/tprobe2.rs`** (new) — scope API lives here:\n  `TProbe2 { name, hist, records }`, plus `TProbe2RecId` (opaque\n  `{ site_id, start_tsc }`, `#[must_use]`, `Copy`) and internal\n  `Record`. `start` / `end` use inline tick reads; `report(&mut\n  self, ...)` drains records before delegating to\n  `band_table::render(\"tprobe2\", …)`. Full tests moved over\n  (`start_end_appends_one_record`, `start_end_preserves_start_tsc`,\n  `start_end_interleaved_non_stack`,\n  `report_drains_records_into_histogram`). Dropped the\n  `record_and_start_end_are_independent` test — irrelevant once\n  `record(ticks)` is no longer on this type. Module-level\n  `#![allow(dead_code)]` with an explanatory comment, pending the\n  first non-test consumer.\n- **`src/main.rs`** — added `mod band_table;` and `mod tprobe2;`.\n- **`src/benches/tp_pc.rs`** — reverted to dev3's hot loop:\n  `let s = ticks::read_ticks(); … let e = ticks::read_ticks();\n  probe.record(e.wrapping_sub(s));`. Dropped `let mut` on the two\n  probe bindings (report is `&self` again). Module doc restored to\n  the pre-dev4 wording.\n- **`Cargo.toml`** — `0.9.0-dev4` → `0.9.0-dev5`.\n\n## Outstanding for dev5\n\n1. **`notes/todo.md`** — add `0.9.0-dev5 — split TProbe2 + revert\n   TProbe to 0.8.0` to `## Done` with reference `[32]`.\n2. **`notes/chores-03.md`** — new section `## Split TProbe2 +\n   revert TProbe (0.9.0-dev5)` with edits list and findings:\n   - Extraction of `band_table::render` (verbatim from dev3 logic —\n     no behavioral change, just relocation).\n   - `TProbe` back to 0.8.0 shape.\n   - `TProbe2` adds the scope API with tests preserved.\n   - `tp-pc` back on the fast path.\n   - Note: user should re-run `iiac-perf tp-pc` on an idle system\n     to confirm dev5 restores the dev3 throughput regime (my\n     post-dev4 smoke measured ≈ 6,258 ns mean min-p99, 781K loops\n     in 5s — slower than dev3's 5,076 ns / 965K loops, likely\n     system-state variance since the hot loop is byte-identical\n     to dev3; worth a cold-system check).\n3. **Checkpoint 1 commit block** — prepare and present for user\n   approval. Title candidate:\n   `refactor: split TProbe2 + revert TProbe to 0.8.0 (0.9.0-dev5)`.\n4. **Checkpoint 2 push + finalize** — on approval.\n\nAfter dev5 lands, 0.9.0 final consolidates: remove `-dev5`, roll\nthe five dev entries into `## Done`, write a `## 0.9.0 release`\nsection in `chores-03.md`.\n\n## Critical files\n\n- `src/band_table.rs` (new)\n- `src/tprobe.rs` (reverted)\n- `src/tprobe2.rs` (new)\n- `src/main.rs` (mod list)\n- `src/benches/tp_pc.rs` (reverted hot loop)\n- `Cargo.toml` (version bump)\n- `notes/todo.md` (+ dev5 Done entry)\n- `notes/chores-03.md` (+ dev5 section)\n\n## Verification\n\n- `cargo fmt --check` → clean.\n- `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` →\n  clean (`TProbe2` module has `#![allow(dead_code)]` — intentional).\n- `cargo test` → 12/12 pass.\n- `cargo test --release` → 12/12 pass.\n- `cargo install --path .` → replaces with `v0.9.0-dev5`.\n- `iiac-perf tp-pc -d 5` (idle system, `--pin` per user's normal\n  setup) → expected: mean min-p99 in the dev3 ≈ 5,000 ns regime\n  with ~960K loops in 5s. If numbers match dev4's 6,400 / 761K,\n  something subtle is still off in the revert — worth a `jj show`\n  side-by-side of `src/benches/tp_pc.rs` at `pvrwunwp` (dev3 commit)\n  vs current.\n"
}

The\nfast path (`TProbe`, direct histogram) is what `tp-pc` needs; the\nscope API (records, order preservation, room for background drain /\nper-site grouping / long-term trace) lives in its own primitive.\n\n## What's done in dev5\n\nAll source edits applied and passing checklist (fmt, clippy `-D\nwarnings`, `cargo test`, `cargo test --release`, `cargo install`,\n`iiac-perf --version → 0.9.0-dev5`):\n\n- **`src/band_table.rs`** (new) — shared tick-valued band-table\n  renderer. `pub(crate) fn render(kind, name, hist, as_ticks)`\n  extracted verbatim from dev3's `TProbe::report` body. Both\n  `TProbe` and `TProbe2` delegate to it, so the table output\n  (header, columns, summary rows) stays identical across the two\n  primitives.\n- **`src/tprobe.rs`** — reverted to its 0.8.0 shape: struct has\n  `{ name, hist }` only; API is `new` + `record(ticks)` +\n  `report(&self, as_ticks)`. No scope API, no records buffer, no\n  dead_code allows. Report delegates to `band_table::render`. Module\n  doc points callers to `tprobe2` for the scope API.\n- **`src/tprobe2.rs`** (new) — scope API lives here:\n  `TProbe2 { name, hist, records }`, plus `TProbe2RecId` (opaque\n  `{ site_id, start_tsc }`, `#[must_use]`, `Copy`) and internal\n  `Record`. `start` / `end` use inline tick reads; `report(&mut\n  self, ...)` drains records before delegating to\n  `band_table::render(\"tprobe2\", …)`. Full tests moved over\n  (`start_end_appends_one_record`, `start_end_preserves_start_tsc`,\n  `start_end_interleaved_non_stack`,\n  `report_drains_records_into_histogram`). Dropped the\n  `record_and_start_end_are_independent` test — irrelevant once\n  `record(ticks)` is no longer on this type. Module-level\n  `#![allow(dead_code)]` with an explanatory comment, pending the\n  first non-test consumer.\n- **`src/main.rs`** — added `mod band_table;` and `mod tprobe2;`.\n- **`src/benches/tp_pc.rs`** — reverted to dev3's hot loop:\n  `let s = ticks::read_ticks(); … let e = ticks::read_ticks();\n  probe.record(e.wrapping_sub(s));`. Dropped `let mut` on the two\n  probe bindings (report is `&self` again). Module doc restored to\n  the pre-dev4 wording.\n- **`Cargo.toml`** — `0.9.0-dev4` → `0.9.0-dev5`.\n\n## Outstanding for dev5\n\n1. **`notes/todo.md`** — add `0.9.0-dev5 — split TProbe2 + revert\n   TProbe to 0.8.0` to `## Done` with reference `[32]`.\n2. **`notes/chores-03.md`** — new section `## Split TProbe2 +\n   revert TProbe (0.9.0-dev5)` with edits list and findings:\n   - Extraction of `band_table::render` (verbatim from dev3 logic —\n     no behavioral change, just relocation).\n   - `TProbe` back to 0.8.0 shape.\n   - `TProbe2` adds the scope API with tests preserved.\n   - `tp-pc` back on the fast path.\n   - Note: user should re-run `iiac-perf tp-pc` on an idle system\n     to confirm dev5 restores the dev3 throughput regime (my\n     post-dev4 smoke measured ≈ 6,258 ns mean min-p99, 781K loops\n     in 5s — slower than dev3's 5,076 ns / 965K loops, likely\n     system-state variance since the hot loop is byte-identical\n     to dev3; worth a cold-system check).\n3. **Checkpoint 1 commit block** — prepare and present for user\n   approval. Title candidate:\n   `refactor: split TProbe2 + revert TProbe to 0.8.0 (0.9.0-dev5)`.\n4. **Checkpoint 2 push + finalize** — on approval.\n\nAfter dev5 lands, 0.9.0 final consolidates: remove `-dev5`, roll\nthe five dev entries into `## Done`, write a `## 0.9.0 release`\nsection in `chores-03.md`.\n\n## Critical files\n\n- `src/band_table.rs` (new)\n- `src/tprobe.rs` (reverted)\n- `src/tprobe2.rs` (new)\n- `src/main.rs` (mod list)\n- `src/benches/tp_pc.rs` (reverted hot loop)\n- `Cargo.toml` (version bump)\n- `notes/todo.md` (+ dev5 Done entry)\n- `notes/chores-03.md` (+ dev5 section)\n\n## Verification\n\n- `cargo fmt --check` → clean.\n- `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` →\n  clean (`TProbe2` module has `#![allow(dead_code)]` — intentional).\n- `cargo test` → 12/12 pass.\n- `cargo test --release` → 12/12 pass.\n- `cargo install --path .` → replaces with `v0.9.0-dev5`.\n- `iiac-perf tp-pc -d 5` (idle system, `--pin` per user's normal\n  setup) → expected: mean min-p99 in the dev3 ≈ 5,000 ns regime\n  with ~960K loops in 5s. 1	//! Bench registry. Each bench module exposes `NAME` (CLI id) and
2	//! `run` (entry point). Add a bench by creating a module and
3	//! appending it to [`REGISTRY`].
4	
5	pub mod min_now;
6	pub mod mpsc_1t;
7	pub mod mpsc_2t;
8	pub mod probe_mpsc_2t;
9	pub mod producer_consumer;
10	pub mod std_now;
11	pub mod tp_pc;
12	
13	use crate::harness::RunCfg;
14	
15	/// Bench entry-point signature.
16	pub type RunFn = fn(&RunCfg);
17	
18	/// Static list of every registered bench, in display order.
19	pub const REGISTRY: &[(&str, RunFn)] = &[
20	    (min_now::NAME, min_now::run),
21	    (std_now::NAME, std_now::run),
22	    (mpsc_1t::NAME, mpsc_1t::run),
23	    (mpsc_2t::NAME, mpsc_2t::run),
24	    (probe_mpsc_2t::NAME, probe_mpsc_2t::run),
25	    (producer_consumer::NAME, producer_consumer::run),
26	    (tp_pc::NAME, tp_pc::run),
27	];
28	
29	/// All registered bench names, in [`REGISTRY`] order. Used for CLI
30	/// help and the `all` resolution.
31	pub fn names() -> Vec<&'static str> {
32	    REGISTRY.iter().map(|(n, _)| *n).collect()
33	}
34	
35	/// Resolve a list of CLI-requested names (or the literal `"all"`)
36	/// to an ordered list of [`RunFn`]s. Returns an error on any
37	/// unknown name.
38	pub fn resolve(requested: &[String]) -> Result<Vec<RunFn>, String> {
39	    let want_all = requested.iter().any(|n| n == "all");
40	    let names: Vec<&str> = if want_all {
41	        names()
42	    } else {
43	        requested.iter().map(|s| s.as_str()).collect()
44	    };
45	
46	    let mut runners = Vec::with_capacity(names.len());
47	    for name in names {
48	        match REGISTRY.iter().find(|(n, _)| *n == name) {
49	            Some((_, run)) => runners.push(*run),
50	            None => {
51	                return Err(format!(
52	                    "unknown bench '{name}'. valid: all, {}",
53	                    self::names().join(", ")
54	                ));
55	            }
56	        }
57	    }
58	    Ok(runners)
59	}
60	
```

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/benches/tp2_pc.rs",
  "content": "//! Scope-API variant of `tp-pc`: same dedicated producer +\n//! consumer threads trading over two `std::sync::mpsc` channels,\n//! but measurement uses [`TProbe2::start`] / [`TProbe2::end`]\n//! rather than the [`TProbe::record`] fast path. Records are\n//! drained into the histogram at `report()` time.\n//!\n//! Run back-to-back with [`crate::benches::tp_pc`] to compare\n//! hot-path cost of the direct-histogram path against the\n//! scope-API path on the same workload, inside one process so\n//! calibration and system state are shared.\n\nuse std::sync::atomic::{AtomicBool, Ordering};\nuse std::sync::{Arc, mpsc};\nuse std::thread;\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\nuse crate::harness::RunCfg;\nuse crate::pin;\nuse crate::tprobe2::TProbe2;\n\n/// Registry name used on the CLI.\npub const NAME: &str = \"tp2-pc\";\n\n/// Registry entry point.\npub fn run(cfg: &RunCfg) {\n    let (req_tx, req_rx) = mpsc::channel::<u64>();\n    let (resp_tx, resp_rx) = mpsc::channel::<u64>();\n    let shutdown = Arc::new(AtomicBool::new(false));\n\n    let producer_cpu = cfg.core_for(0);\n    let consumer_cpu = cfg.core_for(1);\n\n    let producer_shutdown = shutdown.clone();\n    let producer = thread::spawn(move || {\n        pin::pin_current(producer_cpu);\n        let mut probe = TProbe2::new(\"producer loop\");\n        let mut counter: u64 = 0;\n        while !producer_shutdown.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {\n            let id = probe.start(0);\n            counter = counter.wrapping_add(1);\n            if req_tx.send(counter).is_err() {\n                break;\n            }\n            if resp_rx.recv().is_err() {\n                break;\n            }\n            probe.end(id);\n        }\n        probe\n    });\n\n    let consumer = thread::spawn(move || {\n        pin::pin_current(consumer_cpu);\n        let mut probe = TProbe2::new(\"consumer loop\");\n        loop {\n            let id = probe.start(0);\n            let v = match req_rx.recv() {\n                Ok(v) => v,\n                Err(_) => break,\n            };\n            if resp_tx.send(v).is_err() {\n                break;\n            }\n            probe.end(id);\n        }\n        probe\n    });\n\n    thread::sleep(Duration::from_secs_f64(cfg.target_seconds));\n    shutdown.store(true, Ordering::Relaxed);\n\n    let mut producer_probe = producer.join().expect(\"producer panicked\");\n    let mut consumer_probe = consumer.join().expect(\"consumer panicked\");\n\n    println!(\n        \"tp2-pc (2 threads, TProbe2 scope-API) [duration={:.1}s]:\",\n        cfg.target_seconds\n    );\n    producer_probe.report(cfg.report_ticks);\n    consumer_probe.report(cfg.report_ticks);\n}\n"
}
```
