The\n  trade-off surface (cache footprint, future bounded buffer,\n  background drain, per-site grouping) lives here without\n  dragging the fast path into those decisions.\n- `tp_pc` goes back to the fast path (dev3 shape).\n- `tp2_pc` is a new bench — same workload as `tp_pc` but on\n  `TProbe2`. Having both in the registry means `iiac-perf\n  tp-pc tp2-pc -d 5` runs them in one process with shared\n  calibration and thermal state, which is the only honest way\n  to A/B the hot-path cost.\n\nThe dev4 chores section interpreted an across-invocation\ncomparison (dev3 user run → dev4 user run) as a ~21% scope-API\nregression. In-process dev5 measurement (below) shows the real\ndelta is sub-1 %. The earlier gap was dominated by system-state\ndrift, not the scope API.\n\n### Edits\n\n- `Cargo.toml` — version bump to `0.9.0-dev5`.\n- `src/band_table.rs` — **new**, shared `pub(crate) fn\n  render(kind, name, hist, as_ticks)` extracted verbatim from\n  the dev3-era `TProbe::report` body. Both primitives delegate\n  to it so their table output is visually identical.\n- `src/tprobe.rs` — reverted to 0.8.0 shape: `TProbe { name,\n  hist }`, `new` / `record` / `report(&self, as_ticks)`. No\n  scope API, no records buffer, no dead-code allows. Report\n  delegates to `band_table::render`. Module doc points readers\n  to `tprobe2` for the scope API.\n- `src/tprobe2.rs` — **new**, holds `TProbe2 { name, hist,\n  records }`, `TProbe2RecId`, internal `Record`, `new` /\n  `start` / `end` / `report(&mut self, as_ticks)`. Report\n  drains records before delegating to `band_table::render`.\n  Tests moved over (`start_end_appends_one_record`,\n  `start_end_preserves_start_tsc`,\n  `start_end_interleaved_non_stack`,\n  `report_drains_records_into_histogram`); dropped\n  `record_and_start_end_are_independent` — moot once\n  `record(ticks)` isn't on this type.\n- `src/main.rs` — added `mod band_table;` and `mod tprobe2;`.\n- `src/benches/tp_pc.rs` — hot loop reverted to the dev3 shape\n  (`ticks::read_ticks()` pair + `probe.record(e.wrapping_sub(s))`).\n  `let mut` on the two probe bindings dropped (report is\n  `&self` again). Module doc restored to the pre-dev4 wording.\n- `src/benches/tp2_pc.rs` — **new**, registers as CLI\n  `tp2-pc`. Identical structure to `tp_pc` but uses\n  `TProbe2::start(0)` / `TProbe2::end(id)`. `TProbe2`'s first\n  non-test consumer, so no `#![allow(dead_code)]` needed on\n  `tprobe2`.\n- `src/benches/mod.rs` — `pub mod tp2_pc;` + `(tp2_pc::NAME,\n  tp2_pc::run)` in `REGISTRY`.\n- `notes/todo.md` — dev5 Done entry + reference `[32]`.\n- `notes/chores-03.md` — this section.\n\n### Findings\n\n- **In-process A/B is small.** `iiac-perf tp-pc tp2-pc -d 5`\n  on the 3900X (`--pin` default, producer + consumer each on\n  their own core):\n\n  | bench   | loop count | producer mean min-p99 | consumer mean min-p99 |\n  |:--------|-----------:|----------------------:|----------------------:|\n  | tp-pc   | 656,306    | 7,468 ns              | 7,476 ns              |\n  | tp2-pc  | 653,304    | 7,513 ns              | 7,523 ns              |\n\n  Scope API costs ≈ 0.6 % throughput and ≈ 45 ns per iteration\n  against the direct-histogram path. Orders of magnitude\n  smaller than the ~21 % the dev4 chores reported — because\n  dev4's comparison was across two separate invocations with\n  unknown system-state drift between them. This in-process\n  run shares calibration, thermal state, and scheduler\n  pressure; it's the one to trust.\n- **The dev4 \"21 % regression\" narrative was noise.** The\n  Vec-push cost is real but much smaller than raw\n  between-invocation variance on a 3900X. dev4 findings\n  should be read as \"under some conditions scope-API can look\n  slower,\" not \"scope-API *is* 21 % slower.\" The split still\n  stands on API-clarity grounds (two primitives, two use\n  cases, no dead-code juggling) — the perf motivation is\n  weaker than it looked.\n- **band_table extraction is a verbatim move.** The logic in\n  `band_table::render` is the dev3 `TProbe::report` body with\n  `self.hist` → `hist` and `self.name` → `name`. Output\n  shape is unchanged; running tp-pc in dev5 produces the same\n  row layout and column widths as in dev3.\n- **`tprobe2`'s dead-code allow is gone.** `tp2-pc` consumes\n  every pub item on `TProbe2`, so the module-level\n  `#![allow(dead_code)]` we needed in a hypothetical\n  consumer-less split is not required.\n- **Tests green in debug and release.** 12 tests pass (the\n  four `tprobe2::tests::*` and the eight `pin::tests::*`).\n  `tprobe.rs` has no tests — matches its 0.8.0 shape."
}

> AGENT

Striking flip at 60s — tp2-pc is ~22% *faster*, not slower:

| -d 60 | loops | producer min-p99 | consumer min-p99 | per-iter |
|:------|------:|-----------------:|-----------------:|---------:|
| tp-pc  | 12,918,173 | 4,559 ns | 4,558 ns | 4,644 ns |
| tp2-pc | 16,575,811 | 3,536 ns | 3,534 ns | 3,619 ns |

Hypothesis: on the hot path, `hist.record` does bucket-index compute (log-scale) + atomic increment — tens of cycles of real ALU/memory work. `Vec::push` is a 24-byte store + pointer bump, and modern CPUs stream linear writes at tens of GB/s. The growing buffer pays in memory (16.5M × 24 B ≈ 400 MB for a 60s run) but not in cycles until the DRAM bandwidth ceiling, which is well above 276K pushes/s. Once the system reaches steady-state thermal/frequency equilibrium (~5s+ into the run) the scope API wins on throughput outright.

This also retrospectively reframes dev4: it wasn't a scope-API regression, it was cross-invocation system-state drift.

Updating the chores findings to capture the 60s A/B and flip the narrative before the commit.

Small,\n  noise-level.\n\n- **In-process A/B, steady-state runs (-d 60).** Same box,\n  same invocation style, longer duration:\n\n  | bench   | loop count | producer min-p99 | consumer min-p99 | per-iter |\n  |:--------|-----------:|-----------------:|-----------------:|---------:|\n  | tp-pc   | 12,918,173 | 4,559 ns         | 4,558 ns         | 4,644 ns |\n  | tp2-pc  | 16,575,811 | 3,536 ns         | 3,534 ns         | 3,619 ns |\n\n  Scope API is **~22 % faster** throughput and ~1 µs lower\n  per-sample at steady state — the opposite of what dev4\n  suggested. Both benches also run faster at 60 s than at 5 s\n  because the 3900X takes time to reach its boost /\n  thermal / frequency equilibrium; the first few seconds of\n  any run are in a slower regime that doesn't represent\n  long-term cost.\n\n- **Why the scope API wins at steady state.** On the hot\n  path, `hist.record(delta)` does log-scale bucket-index\n  compute + an atomic increment (tens of cycles of real\n  ALU / memory work). `Vec::push(Record)` is a single 24-byte\n  store plus a pointer bump, and modern CPUs stream linear\n  writes at tens of GB/s — far above the 276K pushes/s seen\n  here. The growing buffer (≈ 400 MB over 60 s) pays in\n  memory, not in cycles, until the DRAM bandwidth ceiling,\n  which is well above this workload's rate.\n\n- **The dev4 \"21 % regression\" narrative was noise.** That\n  comparison was across two separate invocations run minutes\n  apart, with different system states (thermal, scheduler\n  load, frequency). Run fairly in one process, the scope API\n  is competitive at short durations and wins at long ones.\n  dev4's chores should be read as \"variance across\n  invocations dwarfs the scope-API delta on this workload,\"\n  not \"scope-API is slower.\"\n\n- **Split stands on API-clarity grounds too.** Even without\n  the steady-state perf win, keeping the primitives separate\n  keeps the fast path and the scope API from trading off on\n  each other's constraints (buffer growth, drain semantics,\n  per-site grouping, &mut self requirements on report)."

Chores-03 dev5 section carries the corrected\nnarrative.\n\n- Cargo.toml: version bump 0.9.0-dev4 → 0.9.0-dev5.\n- src/band_table.rs (new): shared \\`render(kind, name, hist,\n  as_ticks)\\` — verbatim extraction of the dev3 report body.\n- src/tprobe.rs: reverted to 0.8.0 shape; report delegates to\n  band_table.\n- src/tprobe2.rs (new): TProbe2, TProbe2RecId, Record,\n  start/end, drain-in-report; 4 tests moved over.\n- src/main.rs: mod band_table + mod tprobe2.\n- src/benches/tp_pc.rs: reverted to dev3 hot loop.\n- src/benches/tp2_pc.rs (new): TProbe2 mirror of tp-pc,\n  registered as \\`tp2-pc\\`.\n- src/benches/mod.rs: tp2_pc registry entry.\n- notes/todo.md: dev5 Done + reference [32].\n- notes/chores-03.md: \\`## Split TProbe2 + revert TProbe + tp2-pc (0.9.0-dev5)\\`\n  with -d 5 and -d 60 A/B tables and corrected findings.\n\nochid: /.claude/rpkqtpzqpzwp\" \\\n-R .",

wink@3900x 26-04-19T06:55:43.695Z:~/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf ((main))

> AGENT

Fascinating flip — the pinned run reverses the dev5 narrative:

| run                       | tp-pc producer min-p99 | tp2-pc producer min-p99 | winner |
|:--------------------------|-----------------------:|------------------------:|:-------|
| unpinned, -d 60           | 4,559 ns               | 3,536 ns                | tp2-pc ~22% ahead |
| `--pin 5,6,7`, -d 15      | 8,258 ns               | 8,468 ns                | tp-pc ~2.5% ahead |

**Why the flip, hypothesis:** on a 3900X, `--pin 5,6,7` straddles a CCD boundary (core 5 is CCD 0 / CCX 1; cores 6–7 are CCD 1 / CCX 2). Every channel round-trip crosses the Infinity Fabric — hence per-iter blowing up from 4.6 µs to 8.3 µs. But pinning also means the threads *stay* on their cores, so the histogram for `tp-pc` lives hot in L1/L2 continuously — `hist.record` becomes a cheap L1-hit + atomic-inc. When unpinned, the scheduler migrates threads, histogram state goes cold on each migration, and `hist.record` pays cache-miss cost. `tp2-pc`'s `Vec::push` is a streaming-write pattern that's indifferent to cache warmth (DRAM bandwidth is always available), so it wins the unpinned regime but doesn't *gain* anything in the pinned one.

In short:
- **Scope API wins when the fast-path's bucket state can't stay hot** (unpinned, migration-heavy, or very high allocation churn).
- **Fast path wins when the histogram stays L1/L2-resident** (pinned, steady thread-to-core mapping).

This is a much more interesting characterization than either dev4 ("scope slower") or dev5 ("scope faster").

**Where should this finding go?** Three options:

1. **Amend dev5 chores** in place via `jj squash --ignore-immutable` — extends the findings section with this third measurement and the hypothesis.
2. **Ship as part of the 0.9.0 release commit** (the rollup that removes `-dev5`) — natural home for a final observation that spans the whole 0.9.0 arc.
3. **Park in `notes/ideas.md`** — if you want to reserve judgment until more data lands (e.g. same-CCX pin like `--pin 5,4`, dedicated cold-cache vs warm-cache micro, etc.).

My lean: option 2. The release commit already wants a summary of what 0.9.0 delivered and what it learned; this data is exactly that kind of capstone observation. Option 1 works too but immutable-squash is more ceremony for essentially the same result.

Your call.

> DEVELOPER

agree option 2, but have pin being slower but much tighter means, stdev is what we've seen all along so not a surprise to me I run a -d 60 for both for 9 if you think it's a good ieda

> AGENT

Yes, good idea — pinned -d 60 completes a clean 2×2 (short/long × pinned/unpinned) and pins down the story. Also gives data on whether tp2-pc's buffer growth (to ~170 MB at ~7M samples) starts biting once it's firmly past L3.

Suggested run:
```
iiac-perf tp-pc tp2-pc --pin 5,6,7 -d 60
```

If you want an extra data point to isolate the CCD-crossing penalty specifically, a same-CCX pin like `--pin 6,7` would contrast `5,6,7` nicely — same pinned regime, no Infinity Fabric hop — but not essential for the capstone.

Standing by for the numbers.

> DEVELOPER

I was thinking of doing 0,5,10

> AGENT

Worth noting the topology: on 3900X, `--pin 0,5,10` puts producer on core 0 (CCD 0, CCX 0) and consumer on core 5 (CCD 0, CCX 1) — same CCD, cross-CCX, no Infinity Fabric hop. That's a **different regime** from the -d 15 `5,6,7` run (producer on 5 / consumer on 6 crossed CCD). So the comparison at -d 60 would be:

- Unpinned -d 60: scheduler can co-locate same-CCX, fastest available topology.
- Pinned `0,5,10` -d 60: forced cross-CCX, same-CCD — middle ground.
- (The -d 15 `5,6,7` data captures cross-CCD, worst case.)

That's actually a better set of three than repeating `5,6,7` — gives unpinned / moderate-pin / severe-pin rather than unpinned / severe / severe. Go for it.

See the five `-devN`\nsections above for per-step detail. Net delivery:\n\n- `TProbe` (unchanged from 0.8.0): fast-path probe, direct\n  histogram; hot path is `record(ticks)` with a manual tick-read\n  pair on the caller's side.\n- `TProbe2` (new, `src/tprobe2.rs`): scope-API probe;\n  `start(site_id) -> TProbe2RecId` / `end(id)` on the hot path\n  write `(site_id, start_tsc, end_tsc)` records to an internal\n  `Vec<Record>`. Delta math, `max(1)` clamp, and histogram\n  ingestion are deferred to `report()`, which drains the buffer\n  into the histogram before rendering.\n- `band_table::render` (new, `src/band_table.rs`): shared\n  tick-valued band-table renderer — both primitives delegate\n  to it so output is visually identical.\n- `tp-pc` stays on the fast path. `tp2-pc` (new bench) exercises\n  `TProbe2` on the same workload; running\n  `iiac-perf tp-pc tp2-pc -d N` produces an in-process A/B with\n  shared calibration and thermal state.\n\nPer-dev roll-up:\n\n- `-dev1` ✅ plan: TProbe start/end (ideas.md Option B,\n  deferred-processing, minimal hot path).\n- `-dev2` ✅ implement `TProbe::start` / `end` + record buffer\n  + 4 unit tests (on the pre-split TProbe).\n- `-dev3` ✅ lazy `report()` drain: records → histogram.\n- `-dev4` ✅ wired `tp-pc` to `start` / `end`; cross-invocation\n  A/B surfaced what looked like a ~21% regression — later shown\n  to be system-state drift, not scope-API cost (see dev5\n  findings).\n- `-dev5` ✅ physically split: `TProbe` reverted to 0.8.0,\n  `TProbe2` in its own module, `tp2-pc` bench added, fast path\n  restored on `tp-pc`. In-process A/B measurements corrected the\n  dev4 narrative.\n- `0.9.0` final — remove `-dev5`, bump Cargo.toml to `0.9.0`,\n  move the five dev entries + release to todo's `## Done`.\n\n### Capstone performance characterization\n\nThe scope API's hot-path cost vs. the direct-histogram path\ndepends heavily on whether the histogram's state can stay warm\nin cache. Three regimes observed on the 3900X, `iiac-perf tp-pc\ntp2-pc`:\n\n| regime                        | duration | tp-pc loops | tp-pc min-p99 | tp2-pc loops | tp2-pc min-p99 | winner           |\n|:------------------------------|---------:|------------:|--------------:|-------------:|---------------:|:-----------------|\n| unpinned                      | 60 s     | 12,918,173  | 4,559 ns      | 16,575,811   | 3,536 ns       | tp2-pc, ~22 % ↑  |\n| pinned 5,6,7 (cross-CCD)      | 15 s     | 1,789,119   | 8,258 ns      | 1,749,151    | 8,468 ns       | tp-pc, ~2.5 % ↑  |\n| pinned 0,5,10 (cross-CCX, same-CCD) | 60 s | TBD       | TBD           | TBD          | TBD            | TBD              |\n\n(The `0,5,10` row lands once the user runs\n`iiac-perf tp-pc tp2-pc --pin 0,5,10 -d 60`.)\n\nInterpretation:\n\n- **Scope API wins when histogram state can't stay hot.**\n  Unpinned, scheduler migrations cool the histogram's buckets;\n  every `hist.record` pays cache-miss cost + atomic\n  increment. `Vec::push` is a streaming write that doesn't\n  care about cache warmth — modern CPUs sustain tens of GB/s\n  of linear writes, well above the tens of M samples/s this\n  workload produces.\n- **Fast path wins when histogram stays L1/L2-resident.**\n  Pinned, threads stay on their cores and the hot histogram\n  buckets never leave cache; `hist.record` becomes a cheap\n  L1-hit + atomic-inc. `Vec::push` can't beat that — its\n  advantage is \"DRAM-level work at DRAM speed,\" which is only\n  a win vs. a cache-cold histogram.\n- **Cross-CCD pin dominates both.** `--pin 5,6,7` (producer\n  on CCD 0, consumer on CCD 1) adds a ~3.7 µs-per-iter\n  Infinity Fabric hop regardless of which probe is used.\n  Pinning that isolates a CCX without crossing CCDs (the\n  `0,5,10` run) will give a middle ground between unpinned\n  and cross-CCD.\n- **The dev4 \"21 % regression\" was cross-invocation drift.**\n  Two separate runs minutes apart, different thermal /\n  frequency / background-load states — not an apples-to-apples\n  probe comparison. Running both benches in one invocation is\n  the only honest A/B.\n\n### Memory characterization\n\n`TProbe2` keeps every sample as a 24-byte `Record` in an\nunbounded `Vec` until `report()`. At the unpinned -d 60 rate,\nthat's ~400 MB for a single probe. Well inside a typical\nworkstation's RAM but far past L3 — which is fine as long as\nthe write pattern stays streaming (it does). Two mitigations\nare listed as out-of-scope for 0.9.0 and remain so:\n\n- Pre-reserved capacity (`Vec::with_capacity`) to eliminate\n  doubling-realloc tail spikes.\n- Bounded / ring-buffer policy for long-running or unbounded\n  recording.\n\n### When to use which probe\n\n- `TProbe` — single-histogram summary, high sustained sample\n  rate, pinned workloads, or workloads where\n  cache-locality-sensitive hot-path cost matters most.\n- `TProbe2` — need record-order information (TSC timeline), or\n  expect per-site grouping / background drain / trace\n  retention to matter later, or the workload migrates enough\n  that the histogram goes cold between samples.\n\n### Edits\n\n- `Cargo.toml` — version bump `0.9.0-dev5` → `0.9.0`.\n- `notes/todo.md` — `0.9.0` Done entry + reference `[33]`;\n  cleared the In Progress scope-API task.\n- `notes/chores-03.md` — this section.\n\nNo source changes in this final commit; the release marker\nconsolidates the five dev commits.\n"
}
```

> AGENT

Excellent — the third data point actually shifts the interpretation. Let me walk through it:

| regime | tp-pc loops | tp-pc min-p99 | tp2-pc loops | tp2-pc min-p99 | winner | stdev min-p99 (producer) |
|:-------|------------:|--------------:|-------------:|---------------:|:-------|-------------------------:|
| unpinned -d 60 | 12,054,849 | 4,896 ns | 16,088,230 | 3,649 ns | tp2-pc ~25% | ~2,900 ns |
| pin 0,5,10 (same-CCD) -d 60 | 7,316,823 | 8,086 ns | 7,589,776 | 7,806 ns | **tp2-pc ~3.5%** | ~300 ns |
| pin 5,6,7 (cross-CCD) -d 15 | 1,789,119 | 8,258 ns | 1,749,151 | 8,468 ns | tp-pc ~2.5% | ~330 ns |

**Two meaningful observations:**

1. **tp2-pc also wins the same-CCD pinned regime** — +3.5%, tight (stdev 250–300 ns). The "fast path wins when histogram stays L1/L2-resident" hypothesis from my earlier draft was wrong. At steady state, tp2-pc's advantage *shrinks* with pinning but doesn't flip — Vec::push on a streaming buffer beats or ties `hist.record`'s bucket-compute-plus-atomic even when both stay cache-hot.
2. **The cross-CCD 5,6,7 inversion may be duration, not topology.** That's the only 15 s run. The 0,5,10 60 s run gives tp2-pc a small win; unpinned 60 s gives it a big win. A 60 s cross-CCD rerun would tell us whether the short-run result was pre-equilibrium noise. Worth flagging, not worth blocking the release on.

**Pinning dramatically tightens the distribution** (stdev min-p99 drops from ~2,900 ns unpinned → ~300 ns pinned) — consistent with what the 0.6.0 calibration work already captured. Orthogonal to the probe choice.

Updating the capstone table and interpretation with this data.

Running both benches in one invocation is\n  the only honest A/B.",
  "new_string": "Interpretation:\n\n- **Scope API wins at steady state in every regime tested.**\n  Unpinned gives the biggest margin (~25 %) because scheduler\n  migrations cool the histogram buckets and `hist.record`\n  pays cache-miss + atomic cost per sample, while `Vec::push`\n  streams writes to DRAM at bandwidth-unbounded speed. Pinning\n  shrinks the margin because the histogram stays cache-hot —\n  but doesn't invert it: same-CCD pinned 60 s still goes to\n  tp2-pc by ~3.5 %.\n- **Margin shrinks with cache warmth, not with probe\n  complexity.** `Vec::push` of a 24-byte `Record` is\n  consistently cheaper than `hist.record`'s log-scale bucket\n  compute + atomic increment. The gap just becomes less\n  visible once the histogram never leaves L1/L2.\n- **Pinning across CCDs costs ~3 µs per iteration.** The\n  absolute min-p99 numbers jump from ~4,900 ns (unpinned) to\n  ~8,100 ns (same-CCD) and ~8,300 ns (cross-CCD) — the\n  Infinity Fabric hop dominates once threads are locked to\n  different CCDs. The probe choice barely matters at that\n  point.\n- **Pinning dramatically tightens the distribution.** `stdev\n  min-p99` drops from ~2,900 ns unpinned to ~300 ns pinned\n  — an order of magnitude.   "new_string": "### When to use which probe\n\n- `TProbe` — single-histogram summary when memory footprint\n  matters, or the workload generates more samples than the\n  scope-API buffer can afford. The direct-histogram path is\n  bounded in RAM by the fixed HDR histogram size, regardless\n  of sample count.\n- `TProbe2` — default choice for measurement given the\n  steady-state throughput data. Also required when you need\n  record-order information (TSC timeline), or expect per-site\n  grouping / background drain / trace retention to matter\n  later. The hot path is at least as cheap as `TProbe`'s in\n  every regime measured, often meaningfully cheaper; the\n  trade-off is the growing record buffer (24 bytes/sample,\n  unbounded until `report()`)."
}
```

coexist: \`TProbe\` (fast path, direct histogram — unchanged from
0.8.0) and \`TProbe2\` (scope API — \`start\`/\`end\` + deferred
record buffer + drain-in-report). New \`tp2-pc\` bench mirrors
\`tp-pc\` on \`TProbe2\`; \`iiac-perf tp-pc tp2-pc\` is an honest
in-process A/B. The shared band-table renderer lives in
\`src/band_table.rs\`.

Capstone measurement (3900X, 0.9.0, in-process A/B):

| regime                   | duration | tp-pc min-p99 | tp2-pc min-p99 | winner           |
|:-------------------------|---------:|--------------:|---------------:|:-----------------|
| unpinned                 | 60 s     | 4,896 ns      | 3,649 ns       | tp2-pc ~25 % ↑   |
| pin 0,5,10 (same-CCD)    | 60 s     | 8,086 ns      | 7,806 ns       | tp2-pc ~3.5 % ↑  |
| pin 5,6,7 (cross-CCD)    | 15 s     | 8,258 ns      | 8,468 ns       | tp-pc ~2.5 % ↑   |

Scope API wins at steady state in every regime tested;
margin shrinks as the histogram stays cache-hot (pinning)
but doesn't invert. The 15 s cross-CCD row may be
pre-equilibrium noise — flagged as future work. Pinning
orthogonally tightens stdev min-p99 by ~10× (matches the
0.6.0 calibration findings).

430	  `start` / `end` / `report(&mut self, as_ticks)`. Report
431	  drains records before delegating to `band_table::render`.
432	  Tests moved over (`start_end_appends_one_record`,
433	  `start_end_preserves_start_tsc`,
434	  `start_end_interleaved_non_stack`,
435	  `report_drains_records_into_histogram`); dropped
436	  `record_and_start_end_are_independent` — moot once
437	  `record(ticks)` isn't on this type.
438	- `src/main.rs` — added `mod band_table;` and `mod tprobe2;`.
439	- `src/benches/tp_pc.rs` — hot loop reverted to the dev3 shape
440	  (`ticks::read_ticks()` pair + `probe.record(e.wrapping_sub(s))`).
441	  `let mut` on the two probe bindings dropped (report is
442	  `&self` again). Module doc restored to the pre-dev4 wording.
443	- `src/benches/tp2_pc.rs` — **new**, registers as CLI
444	  `tp2-pc`. Identical structure to `tp_pc` but uses
445	  `TProbe2::start(0)` / `TProbe2::end(id)`. `TProbe2`'s first
446	  non-test consumer, so no `#![allow(dead_code)]` needed on
447	  `tprobe2`.
448	- `src/benches/mod.rs` — `pub mod tp2_pc;` + `(tp2_pc::NAME,
449	  tp2_pc::run)` in `REGISTRY`.
450	- `notes/todo.md` — dev5 Done entry + reference `[32]`.
451	- `notes/chores-03.md` — this section.
452	
453	### Findings
454	
455	- **In-process A/B, short runs (-d 5).** `iiac-perf tp-pc
456	  tp2-pc -d 5` on the 3900X, `--pin` default:
457	
458	  | bench   | loop count | producer min-p99 | consumer min-p99 |
459	  |:--------|-----------:|-----------------:|-----------------:|
460	  | tp-pc   | 656,306    | 7,468 ns         | 7,476 ns         |
461	  | tp2-pc  | 653,304    | 7,513 ns         | 7,523 ns         |
462	
463	  Scope API ≈ 0.6 % slower throughput at 5 s. Small,
464	  noise-level.
465	
466	- **In-process A/B, steady-state runs (-d 60).** Same box,
467	  same invocation style, longer duration:
468	
469	  | bench   | loop count | producer min-p99 | consumer min-p99 | per-iter |
470	  |:--------|-----------:|-----------------:|-----------------:|---------:|
471	  | tp-pc   | 12,918,173 | 4,559 ns         | 4,558 ns         | 4,644 ns |
472	  | tp2-pc  | 16,575,811 | 3,536 ns         | 3,534 ns         | 3,619 ns |
473	
474	  Scope API is **~22 % faster** throughput and ~1 µs lower
475	  per-sample at steady state — the opposite of what dev4
476	  suggested. Both benches also run faster at 60 s than at 5 s
477	  because the 3900X takes time to reach its boost /
478	  thermal / frequency equilibrium; the first few seconds of
479	  any run are in a slower regime that doesn't represent
480	  long-term cost.
481	
482	- **Why the scope API wins at steady state.** On the hot
483	  path, `hist.record(delta)` does log-scale bucket-index
484	  compute + an atomic increment (tens of cycles of real
485	  ALU / memory work). `Vec::push(Record)` is a single 24-byte
486	  store plus a pointer bump, and modern CPUs stream linear
487	  writes at tens of GB/s — far above the 276K pushes/s seen
488	  here. The growing buffer (≈ 400 MB over 60 s) pays in
489	  memory, not in cycles, until the DRAM bandwidth ceiling,
490	  which is well above this workload's rate.
491	
492	- **The dev4 "21 % regression" narrative was noise.** That
493	  comparison was across two separate invocations run minutes
494	  apart, with different system states (thermal, scheduler
495	  load, frequency). Run fairly in one process, the scope API
496	  is competitive at short durations and wins at long ones.
497	  dev4's chores should be read as "variance across
498	  invocations dwarfs the scope-API delta on this workload,"
499	  not "scope-API is slower."
500	
501	- **Split stands on API-clarity grounds too.** Even without
502	  the steady-state perf win, keeping the primitives separate
503	  keeps the fast path and the scope API from trading off on
504	  each other's constraints (buffer growth, drain semantics,
505	  per-site grouping, &mut self requirements on report).
506	- **band_table extraction is a verbatim move.** The logic in
507	  `band_table::render` is the dev3 `TProbe::report` body with
508	  `self.hist` → `hist` and `self.name` → `name`. Output
509	  shape is unchanged; running tp-pc in dev5 produces the same
510	  row layout and column widths as in dev3.
511	- **`tprobe2`'s dead-code allow is gone.** `tp2-pc` consumes
512	  every pub item on `TProbe2`, so the module-level
513	  `#![allow(dead_code)]` we needed in a hypothetical
514	  consumer-less split is not required.
515	- **Tests green in debug and release.** 12 tests pass (the
516	  four `tprobe2::tests::*` and the eight `pin::tests::*`).
517	  `tprobe.rs` has no tests — matches its 0.8.0 shape.
518	
519	## 0.9.0 release: TProbe2 scope API + tp2-pc (0.9.0)

<tool_use_error>String to replace not found in file.
String: Observations:

- **tp2-pc wins both 60-second regimes measured.** Unpinned
  60 s: ~25 % margin. Same-CCD pin `0,5,10` 60 s: ~3.5 %
  margin. The only measurement where tp-pc leads is the
  15-second `5,6,7` cross-CCD run (~2.5 %).
- **Absolute min-p99 jumps with pinning.** From ~4,900 ns
  (unpinned) to ~8,100 ns (same-CCD) and ~8,300 ns
  (cross-CCD). Most of the delta is unpinned→pinned
  (~3 µs); crossing CCDs on top adds ~200 ns.
- **Pinning tightens the distribution.** `stdev min-p99`
  drops from ~2,900 ns unpinned to ~300 ns pinned — about
  an order of magnitude. Orthogonal to probe choice; the
  0.6.0 calibration chores already characterized this for
  framing.
- **Caveat: the `5,6,7` row is the only 15-second run.** A
  60-second rerun of that pin would tell us whether the
  tp-pc 2.5 % lead there is a cross-CCD topology effect or a
  pre-equilibrium artifact (the 0.6.0 chores flagged short
  runs as vulnerable to boost/thermal ramp). Listed as
  future work, not blocking this release.
- **The dev4 "21 % regression" was cross-invocation drift.**
  Two separate runs minutes apart, different thermal /
  frequency / background-load states — not an
  apples-to-apples probe comparison. Running both benches in
  one invocation is the only honest A/B and gives the
  numbers above.

A mechanism-level explanation (why scope API wins at steady
state, why the margin varies) would need microarch
measurements — L1/L2 miss rates on the histogram, atomic
contention, DRAM bandwidth saturation on the record buffer,
etc. The data above shows the *what*; the *why* is
speculative without those.</tool_use_error>
```

577	Observations:
578	
579	- **tp2-pc wins both 60-second regimes measured.** Unpinned
580	  60 s: ~25 % margin. Same-CCD pin `0,5,10` 60 s: ~3.5 %
581	  margin. The only measurement where tp-pc leads is the
582	  15-second `5,6,7` cross-CCD run (~2.5 %).
583	- **Absolute min-p99 jumps with pinning.** From ~4,900 ns
584	  (unpinned) to ~8,100 ns (same-CCD pin) and ~8,300 ns
585	  (cross-CCD pin). Most of the delta is unpinned→pinned
586	  (~3 µs); crossing CCDs on top adds ~200 ns.
587	- **Pinning tightens the distribution.** `stdev min-p99`
588	  drops from ~2,900 ns unpinned to ~300 ns pinned — about
589	  an order of magnitude. Orthogonal to probe choice; the
590	  0.6.0 calibration chores already characterized this for
591	  framing.
592	- **Caveat: the `5,6,7` row is the only 15-second run.** A
593	  60-second rerun of that pin would tell us whether the
594	  tp-pc 2.5 % lead there is a cross-CCD topology effect or a
595	  pre-equilibrium artifact (the 0.6.0 chores flagged short
596	  runs as vulnerable to boost/thermal ramp). Listed as
597	  future work, not blocking this release.
598	- **The dev4 "21 % regression" was cross-invocation drift.**
599	  Two separate runs minutes apart, different thermal /
600	  frequency / background-load states — not an
601	  apples-to-apples probe comparison. Running both benches in
602	  one invocation is the only honest A/B and gives the
603	  numbers above.
604	
605	A mechanism-level explanation (why scope API wins at steady
606	state, why the margin varies) would need microarch
607	measurements — L1/L2 miss rates on the histogram, atomic
608	contention, DRAM bandwidth saturation on the record buffer,
609	etc. The data above shows the *what*; the *why* is
610	speculative without those.
611	
612	### Memory characterization
613	
614	`TProbe2` keeps every sample as a 24-byte `Record` in an
615	unbounded `Vec` until `report()`. At the unpinned -d 60 rate,
616	that's ~400 MB for a single probe. Well inside a typical
617	workstation's RAM but far past L3 — which is fine as long as
618	the write pattern stays streaming (it does). Two mitigations
619	are listed as out-of-scope for 0.9.0 and remain so:
620	
621	- Pre-reserved capacity (`Vec::with_capacity`) to eliminate
622	  doubling-realloc tail spikes.
623	- Bounded / ring-buffer policy for long-running or unbounded
624	  recording.

Orthogonal to probe choice; the\n  0.6.0 calibration chores already characterized this for\n  framing.\n- **Caveat: the `5,6,7` row is the only 15-second run.** A\n  60-second rerun of that pin would tell us whether the\n  tp-pc 2.5 % lead there is a cross-CCD topology effect or a\n  pre-equilibrium artifact (the 0.6.0 chores flagged short\n  runs as vulnerable to boost/thermal ramp). Listed as\n  future work, not blocking this release.\n- **The dev4 \"21 % regression\" was cross-invocation drift.**\n  Two separate runs minutes apart, different thermal /\n  frequency / background-load states — not an\n  apples-to-apples probe comparison. Running both benches in\n  one invocation is the only honest A/B and gives the\n  numbers above.",
  "new_string": "- **tp2-pc wins or ties all three 60-second regimes.**\n  Unpinned: ~25 % margin. Same-CCD pin (`0,5,10`): ~3.5 %\n  margin. Cross-CCD pin (`5,6,7`): ~0.5 % margin — within\n  one stdev, statistical tie. No regime at 60 s shows tp-pc\n  ahead.\n- **Pinning absolute min-p99 goes up, spread goes down.**\n  Unpinned producer min-p99 ≈ 4,900 ns with stdev ≈\n  2,900 ns; pinned (either topology) ≈ 8,100 ns with stdev\n  ≈ 320 ns. Absolute latency nearly 2× higher, stdev about\n  an order of magnitude tighter. The 0.6.0 calibration\n  chores already characterized the tightening for framing;\n  probe measurements reconfirm it. Orthogonal to probe\n  choice.\n- **Cross-CCD vs same-CCD is small at 60 s.** Absolute tp-pc\n  min-p99 is 8,086 ns (same-CCD) vs 8,140 ns (cross-CCD)\n  — ~50 ns apart, loop counts within ~0.7 %. Most of the\n  unpinned→pinned jump happens on any thread separation; the\n  additional CCD crossing is a minor adder at this workload.\n- **The dev4 \"21 % regression\" was cross-invocation drift.**\n  Two separate runs minutes apart, different thermal /\n  frequency / background-load states — not an\n  apples-to-apples probe comparison. Running both benches in\n  one invocation is the only honest A/B and gives the\n  numbers above."
}

Data-driven guidance:

- `TProbe` — when memory footprint is the binding constraint.

pinning regime) would be needed to make a stronger

Put\ndurable context in CLAUDE.md (or committed `notes/`) instead.\n\n## Speculation marker\n\nDurable text the bot writes — CLAUDE.md, `notes/`, commit\nbodies, chores sections — should stick to observations and\ndirect descriptions of the code or data. If a mechanism,\nhypothesis, or causal claim enters the text, prefix it with\n\"The bot thinks ...\" so a reader can tell the measured from\nthe inferred.\n\n**Why:** unmarked speculation reads like evidence, and a future\nreader (or the bot on a later session) can pick it up as a\nknown fact when it's not. Measured / inferred is a distinction\nworth keeping visible in the written record.\n\n**How to apply:** observations and factual descriptions need no\nmarker. 326	    instead of `ticks::read_ticks`.
327	- `notes/todo.md` — dev4 Done entry + reference `[31]`.
328	- `notes/chores-03.md` — this section.
329	
330	### Findings
331	
332	- **A/B vs. dev3: dev4 is slower per-sample and completes
333	  fewer iterations.** `iiac-perf tp-pc` (default `-d 5`) on
334	  the 3900X (idle, `--pin` default):
335	
336	  | version | producer mean min-p99 | consumer mean min-p99 | loop count (5 s) |
337	  |--------:|----------------------:|----------------------:|-----------------:|
338	  | dev3    | ≈ 5,076 ns            | ≈ 5,079 ns            | 965,279          |
339	  | dev4    | ≈ 6,423 ns            | ≈ 6,426 ns            | 761,259          |
340	
341	  dev4 adds ~1,350 ns per iteration (mean min-p99) and loses
342	  ~21 % of throughput. The per-iter overhead and the
343	  throughput loss are consistent (wall-time per iter:
344	  ≈ 5,180 ns dev3 vs ≈ 6,570 ns dev4 — matches the min-p99
345	  delta).
346	
347	  Likely cause: `record(ticks)` wrote one atomic-increment into
348	  a bucket that fits in L1; the new path stores 24 bytes per
349	  sample into a growing `Vec<Record>` that reaches ~17 MB over
350	  5 s of recording — swamps L2 and triggers doubling
351	  reallocations. The Vec::push itself is cheap in isolation;
352	  the cumulative cache footprint + realloc memcpys are what
353	  bite in a high-rate loop.
354	
355	  The earlier `-d 0.5` smoke showed dev4 *faster* — that was a
356	  shorter run where the buffer never grew into its
357	  cache-pressure regime. The 5 s run is the representative
358	  measurement.
359	- **Scope API is currently a flexibility-for-throughput
360	  trade.** What it buys: record-order info (TSC timeline,
361	  not just a histogram), optional per-site grouping later,
362	  optional background drain / ring buffer without API
363	  change, and a path to long-term trace export. What it
364	  costs today: high-rate hot-path throughput vs. the direct
365	  `record(ticks)` path. Mitigations are already listed as
366	  out-of-scope for 0.9.0 (pre-reserved capacity, ring
367	  buffer, background drain, per-site grouping); pick them up
368	  once a use case pulls.
369	- **Low-tail samples.** Both dev3 and dev4 show a `min-p1`
370	  band starting in the tens of ns (dev3: 10/50 ns; dev4:
371	  10/60 ns), consistent with round-trips that complete on
372	  an already-buffered channel (`recv` short-circuits without
373	  going to sleep). Not a regression.
374	- **Leaked ids on shutdown paths.** Both producer and
375	  consumer loops call `probe.start(0)`, then do channel I/O
376	  that can break on `Err`, and only call `probe.end(id)` on
377	  the happy path. On break paths the id is dropped without a
378	  matching `end`, which per Option-B design just means the
379	  in-flight sample is lost — no slot reservation to clean
380	  up, no panic. The buffer invariant (only complete records)
381	  is preserved.
382	- **`record(ticks)` retained deliberately.** For high-rate
383	  probes the direct-histogram path is currently the
384	  performance-preferred choice; keeping `record(ticks)`
385	  around gives callers that knob without forcing them onto
386	  the scope API. The `#[allow(dead_code)]` on it notes this
387	  pending a later decision.
388	- **Tests green in both debug and release.** All 13 still
389	  pass (`cargo test` and `cargo test --release`).
390

392	
393	Physically splits the two recording primitives that had been
394	fighting each other on `TProbe`:
395

519	
520	Ships the 0.9.0 "scope-API probe" pass. See the five `-devN`
521	sections above for per-step detail. Net delivery:
522	
523	- `TProbe` (unchanged from 0.8.0): fast-path probe, direct
524	  histogram; hot path is `record(ticks)` with a manual tick-read
525	  pair on the caller's side.
526	- `TProbe2` (new, `src/tprobe2.rs`): scope-API probe;
527	  `start(site_id) -> TProbe2RecId` / `end(id)` on the hot path
528	  write `(site_id, start_tsc, end_tsc)` records to an internal
529	  `Vec<Record>`. Delta math, `max(1)` clamp, and histogram
530	  ingestion are deferred to `report()`, which drains the buffer
531	  into the histogram before rendering.
532	- `band_table::render` (new, `src/band_table.rs`): shared
533	  tick-valued band-table renderer — both primitives delegate
534	  to it so output is visually identical.
535	- `tp-pc` stays on the fast path. `tp2-pc` (new bench) exercises
536	  `TProbe2` on the same workload; running
537	  `iiac-perf tp-pc tp2-pc -d N` produces an in-process A/B with
538	  shared calibration and thermal state.
539	
540	Per-dev roll-up:
541	
542	- `-dev1` ✅ plan: TProbe start/end (ideas.md Option B,
543	  deferred-processing, minimal hot path).
544	- `-dev2` ✅ implement `TProbe::start` / `end` + record buffer
545	  + 4 unit tests (on the pre-split TProbe).
546	- `-dev3` ✅ lazy `report()` drain: records → histogram.
547	- `-dev4` ✅ wired `tp-pc` to `start` / `end`; cross-invocation
548	  A/B surfaced what looked like a ~21% regression — later shown
549	  to be system-state drift, not scope-API cost (see dev5
550	  findings).
551	- `-dev5` ✅ physically split: `TProbe` reverted to 0.8.0,
552	  `TProbe2` in its own module, `tp2-pc` bench added, fast path
553	  restored on `tp-pc`. In-process A/B measurements corrected the
554	  dev4 narrative.
555	- `0.9.0` final — remove `-dev5`, bump Cargo.toml to `0.9.0`,
556	  move the five dev entries + release to todo's `## Done`.
557	
558	### Capstone performance characterization
559	
560	The scope API's hot-path cost vs. the direct-histogram path
561	varies by pinning regime. Three measurements on the 3900X,
562	`iiac-perf tp-pc tp2-pc`:
563	
564	| regime                              | duration | tp-pc loops | tp-pc min-p99 | tp2-pc loops | tp2-pc min-p99 | stdev min-p99 (producer) | winner                |
565	|:------------------------------------|---------:|------------:|--------------:|-------------:|---------------:|-------------------------:|:----------------------|
566	| unpinned                            | 60 s     | 12,054,849  | 4,896 ns      | 16,088,230   | 3,649 ns       | ~2,900 ns                | tp2-pc, ~25 % ↑       |
567	| pinned 0,5,10 (cross-CCX, same-CCD) | 60 s     |  7,316,823  | 8,086 ns      |  7,589,776   | 7,806 ns       | ~300 ns                  | tp2-pc, ~3.5 % ↑      |
568	| pinned 5,6,7 (cross-CCD)            | 60 s     |  7,267,058  | 8,140 ns      |  7,311,354   | 8,107 ns       | ~330 ns                  | tp2-pc, ~0.5 % ↑ (tie) |
569	
570	All three rows captured on 0.9.0 at -d 60. A 15-second version
571	of the `5,6,7` run during dev5 showed tp-pc ahead by ~2.5 %;
572	the 60-second version flips it to a statistical tie — which the
573	bot thinks is evidence that the shorter run was
574	pre-equilibrium noise, but not proof.
575	
576	Observations:
577	
578	- **tp2-pc wins or ties all three 60-second regimes.**
579	  Unpinned: ~25 % margin. Same-CCD pin (`0,5,10`): ~3.5 %
580	  margin. Cross-CCD pin (`5,6,7`): ~0.5 % margin — within
581	  one stdev, statistical tie. No regime at 60 s shows tp-pc
582	  ahead.
583	- **Pinning absolute min-p99 goes up, spread goes down.**
584	  Unpinned producer min-p99 ≈ 4,900 ns with stdev ≈
585	  2,900 ns; pinned (either topology) ≈ 8,100 ns with stdev
586	  ≈ 320 ns. Absolute latency nearly 2× higher, stdev about
587	  an order of magnitude tighter. The 0.6.0 calibration
588	  chores already characterized the tightening for framing;
589	  probe measurements reconfirm it. Orthogonal to probe
590	  choice.
591	- **Cross-CCD vs same-CCD is small at 60 s.** Absolute tp-pc
592	  min-p99 is 8,086 ns (same-CCD) vs 8,140 ns (cross-CCD)
593	  — ~50 ns apart, loop counts within ~0.7 %. Most of the
594	  unpinned→pinned jump happens on any thread separation; the
595	  additional CCD crossing is a minor adder at this workload.
596	- **The dev4 "21 % regression" was cross-invocation drift.**
597	  Two separate runs minutes apart, different thermal /
598	  frequency / background-load states — not an
599	  apples-to-apples probe comparison. Running both benches in
600	  one invocation is the only honest A/B and gives the
601	  numbers above.
602	
603	A mechanism-level explanation (why scope API wins at steady
604	state, why the margin shrinks with pinning, what drives the
605	~50 ns cross-CCD adder) would need microarch measurements —
606	L1/L2 miss rates, atomic contention, DRAM bandwidth
607	saturation on the record buffer, Infinity Fabric latency
608	counters. The data above shows the *what*; the *why* is
609	speculative without those.
610	
611	### Memory characterization
612	
613	`TProbe2` keeps every sample as a 24-byte `Record` in an
614	unbounded `Vec` until `report()`. At the unpinned -d 60 rate,
615	that's ~400 MB for a single probe. Well inside a typical
616	workstation's RAM but far past L3 — which is fine as long as
617	the write pattern stays streaming (it does). Two mitigations
618	are listed as out-of-scope for 0.9.0 and remain so:
619	
620	- Pre-reserved capacity (`Vec::with_capacity`) to eliminate
621	  doubling-realloc tail spikes.
622	- Bounded / ring-buffer policy for long-running or unbounded
623	  recording.
624	
625	### When to use which probe
626	
627	Data-driven guidance:
628	
629	- `TProbe` — when memory footprint is the binding constraint.
630	  RAM use is bounded by the fixed HDR histogram size
631	  regardless of sample count, so very long or very high-rate
632	  recording doesn't grow.
633	- `TProbe2` — default choice for new measurement code. Ties
634	  or wins tp-pc on throughput in every 60-second regime
635	  measured (unpinned +25 %, same-CCD +3.5 %, cross-CCD tie),
636	  so no hot-path reason to prefer the direct-histogram path.
637	  Required when you need record-order information (TSC
638	  timeline), or expect per-site grouping / background drain /
639	  trace retention to matter later. Cost is the growing record
640	  buffer (24 bytes per sample, unbounded until `report()`).
641	
642	### Edits
643	
644	- `Cargo.toml` — version bump `0.9.0-dev5` → `0.9.0`.
645	- `notes/todo.md` — `0.9.0` Done entry + reference `[33]`;
646	  cleared the In Progress scope-API task.
647	- `notes/chores-03.md` — this section.
648	
649	No source changes in this final commit; the release marker
650	consolidates the five dev commits.
651	
```

jj commit -m \
"feat: 0.9.0 release — TProbe2 + tp2-pc (0.9.0)" \

coexist: \`TProbe\` (fast path, direct histogram — unchanged from
0.8.0) and \`TProbe2\` (scope API — \`start\`/\`end\` + deferred
record buffer + drain-in-report). New \`tp2-pc\` bench mirrors
\`tp-pc\` on \`TProbe2\`; \`iiac-perf tp-pc tp2-pc\` is an honest
in-process A/B. Shared band-table renderer lives in
\`src/band_table.rs\`.

Capstone measurement (3900X, 0.9.0, in-process A/B, all at
-d 60):

| regime                     | tp-pc min-p99 | tp2-pc min-p99 | winner            |
|:---------------------------|--------------:|---------------:|:------------------|
| unpinned                   | 4,896 ns      | 3,649 ns       | tp2-pc ~25 % ↑    |
| pin 0,5,10 (same-CCD)      | 8,086 ns      | 7,806 ns       | tp2-pc ~3.5 % ↑   |
| pin 5,6,7 (cross-CCD)      | 8,140 ns      | 8,107 ns       | tp2-pc ~0.5 % (tie) |

tp2-pc wins or ties every 60-second regime tested. Pinning
adds ~3 µs absolute min-p99 and tightens stdev ~10×; the
extra CCD crossing adds only ~50 ns over same-CCD pinning.
The dev4 '21% regression' was cross-invocation system-state
drift, not probe cost.
